## [Temporal Validity in Code Assistants: MemStrata Eliminates Stale-Fact Errors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20685) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper validates MemStrata, a deterministic supersession memory approach, on real GitHub fixes, achieving 0.91 answer accuracy versus RAG's 0.57-0.59, and reducing stale-fact serving to ~0%. This addresses a critical limitation of RAG in coding assistants—the inability to track temporal changes—which can lead to serving outdated code snippets. The significant accuracy improvement suggests a promising direction for more reliable AI-assisted software development. The study uses 130 clean atomic state transitions extracted from 707 real GitHub issues (SWE-bench Lite + Verified). MemStrata operates at RAG retrieval latency (~2.1 s) versus ~18 s for an LLM reranker, and the authors note that only ~18% of real fixes are clean atomic transitions, with extraction coverage deferred to future work.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that combines large language models with external knowledge retrieval to improve answer accuracy. However, RAG lacks a model of time, so when a fact changes (e.g., a function is renamed), it may retrieve both old and new values and cannot determine which is current, leading to stale-fact errors. MemStrata is a deterministic memory system that tracks (subject, relation, object) triples and supersedes outdated facts, ensuring that only the current value is served.

## [Vis-Poison: Visual Knowledge Poisoning Attack on Multimodal RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20756) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Vis-Poison is a novel attack that poisons visual knowledge in multimodal retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems by embedding malicious payloads directly into images, without altering any text. It achieves an end-to-end black-box attack success rate of 40.16% to 65.40% against 30k-entry knowledge bases. This attack highlights a critical security vulnerability in multimodal RAG systems, which are increasingly used in real-world applications. It demonstrates that visual knowledge can be exploited even in black-box settings, posing a significant threat to the integrity and reliability of MLLM outputs. The attack is automated via a multi-agent method that constructs visually plausible poisoned images, and it operates purely at the semantic level, making it model-agnostic. It remains effective against various MLLMs that can answer correctly from parametric knowledge alone, with an average success rate above 60%.

Prior attacks often relied on altering textual metadata, but Vis-Poison shows that the image content itself can be the attack vector.

## [CARD Diagnoses Belief-to-Action Routing Failures in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20763) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CARD (Cross-Axis Routing Diagnostic), a method that steers activations along one axis (e.g., belief) and measures the response of another axis's prediction (e.g., action). Applied to open-weight VLMs on a new cooperative grid-world benchmark called Relay Chain, it reveals a critical routing failure: models fail to incorporate belief representations into next action predictions. This finding is significant because it shows that even though VLMs internally represent mental states like beliefs, they fail to use these representations in downstream action predictions, which could undermine their reliability in cooperative and interactive tasks. The CARD diagnostic and Relay Chain benchmark provide tools for the community to further investigate and improve VLM reasoning and safety. CARD works in three steps: identify a steering vector for a mental representation in one axis, steer the model in that direction, and measure the prediction of a different axis. The Relay Chain benchmark is a cooperative grid-world environment designed to test belief-to-action routing. The study focuses on open-weight VLMs, and the observed failure suggests that belief information is left unused in action prediction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both visual and textual information. Recent research using linear probes and activation steering has shown that VLMs internally represent mental states such as agents' beliefs, knowledge, and intentions. However, it was unclear whether these representations are actually used in downstream predictions. CARD addresses this gap by providing a diagnostic method to test cross-axis routing, and Relay Chain offers a benchmark to evaluate such routing in cooperative tasks.

## [CertVLA: Certified Defense for VLA Models Against Physical Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20791) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CertVLA introduces a certified defense for Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models against bounded physical patch and texture attacks, providing provable robustness guarantees for closed-loop control. It proposes a calibrated region of behaviorally consistent actions and deterministic covering masks to ensure at least one checked prediction is attack-free. This work addresses a critical gap in extending certified defenses to continuous, temporally correlated actions in embodied AI, which is essential for the security of real-world robotic systems. It provides theoretical guarantees that are independent of patch content and physical transformations, making it a significant step toward trustworthy VLA deployment. CertVLA normalizes action disagreement by benign variation of each mask pair and accepts a single-mask anchor only when consistent under every second mask. It calibrates the max-min-max episode score for finite-sample clean coverage and extends the certificate to complete closed-loop rollouts via query-level decisions.

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that integrate vision, language, and action, enabling robots to execute tasks from visual and textual instructions. Physical patch attacks are localized, visible perturbations that can mislead models in the real world, and existing certified defenses typically focus on discrete labels rather than continuous actions.

## [Periodic Row-wise Muon Cuts Overhead for Large Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20818) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Periodic Row-wise Muon, a modification to the Muon optimizer that performs a full Newton-Schulz spectral update only every K steps and uses a low-cost row-wise update in between, reducing computational and communication overhead for large Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) from 1.3B to 15B parameters. This work addresses a key bottleneck in scaling Muon optimizers for large DiTs, showing that Periodic Row-wise Muon preserves Muon's generative quality advantage (12.9-19.1% improvement over AdamW) while cutting optimizer time by up to 54.3% and end-to-end step time by up to 24.3%, making efficient large-scale training more feasible. The method reduces logical communication volume by 66.7% and reaches best generative quality with 33.7-64.8% less active training time. The distributed implementation operates on sharded momentum during non-refresh steps and accelerates spectral refreshes via bucketed all-gather and communication-computation overlap.

**Background**: Muon is a matrix-aware optimizer that balances updates across singular directions, improving training of large models. However, its standard implementation requires a 5-step Newton-Schulz iteration (NS5) at every optimization step, which is computationally expensive. Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are a class of diffusion models using transformer backbones, widely used for image and video generation.

AdvA preserves objective-wise temporal credit before worst-objective-aware nonlinear scalarisation and adds a residual correction to policy updates. The method was evaluated against Reward-PPO and a feedforward-plus-PID baseline under nominal operation, measurement uncertainties, and unseen initial equilibria, and it was the only learned controller to complete the horizon under combined uncertainties.

Reinforcement learning offers a way to handle the complex multi-objective control problem.

MentorPulse introduces a training-free refresh mechanism for cross-model latent guidance that updates the mentor's memory every 16 tokens without resetting the student's KV cache, restoring a 2.0-point gain over the no-guidance baseline in multi-turn instruction following. It compresses mentor states into a capped slot memory and uses gated cross-attention to keep guidance fresh at practical cost. This addresses a critical limitation in cross-model latent guidance for long-form generation, where static guidance degrades performance. By enabling efficient refresh, MentorPulse improves constraint satisfaction and closes 52.2% of the mentor-student gap across thirteen datasets, outperforming existing methods and offering a practical solution for efficient LLM inference. MentorPulse uses a capped slot memory to compress mentor states and processes newly generated tokens incrementally, updating the memory read by the student without resetting its KV cache. It performs best on all eleven mentor-student pairs from three model families, with margins narrowing as the capability gap grows, and a lightweight read-pattern check predicts the gain before deployment.

The paper introduces Quantization-Aware Healing (QAH), a distillation-based method that recovers 4-bit compressed LLMs by distilling from the original uncompressed model. On a GPT-OSS 120B to 60B to MXFP4 pipeline, the QAH student matches or beats its bfloat16 source on 7 of 9 benchmarks, and is released open-weight as Hypernova-60B. It could significantly impact model serving practices by enabling cheaper, high-quality compressed models. **Background**: Quantization-aware training (QAT) is a common technique that simulates low-precision effects during training to make models robust to quantization. Model distillation transfers knowledge from a larger teacher model to a smaller student model. This paper combines these ideas by distilling from the original uncompressed model instead of using hard labels, which helps recover the performance of compressed and quantized models.

## [Security-Aware Prompts Reduce LLM-Generated Web App Vulnerabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20963) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A controlled study on arXiv (2608.20963) generated six web applications with an agentic coding assistant, each in baseline and security-aware prompt variants, finding that security-aware prompts produced fewer confirmed vulnerabilities (24 vs 51) and no Critical or High issues. This study provides early evidence that explicitly requesting security best practices in prompts can significantly improve the security of LLM-generated code, which is increasingly used in real-world applications. It highlights the importance of prompt engineering as a practical mitigation for AI-generated software vulnerabilities. The study used a single, non-iterative generation round with the same agentic coding assistant and model version, and analyzed all twelve programs using static, dependency, dynamic, and manual testing, yielding 75 confirmed findings out of 85 candidates. The authors note the corpus is small and each variant was generated once, so they report descriptive observations rather than statistically established effects.

The testing techniques include static analysis, dependency analysis, dynamic testing, and manual testing, which together provide a comprehensive security assessment.

## [LLMs Show Conditional Competence but Fragility in Cybersecurity Defense Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20966) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates large language models (LLMs) on structured security reasoning tasks involving attack graph defense selection, finding they can produce coherent strategies close to a game-theoretic baseline when explicit structure is provided, but their performance is fragile and sensitive to prompt changes. This research is significant because it provides empirical evidence on the reliability of LLMs in cybersecurity decision-making, highlighting that while they can approximate structured reasoning under controlled conditions, they do not apply it robustly. This has important implications for the design and evaluation of AI-assisted security decision-support systems, as over-reliance on LLMs could lead to suboptimal or unsafe security choices. The study uses attack graphs derived from real-world threat scenarios including ransomware, supply-chain compromise, cloud abuse, Kubernetes attacks, POS malware, and ICS/OT intrusion. LLMs were compared against a game-theoretic optimization baseline, and results showed that small prompt changes can substantially alter rankings, and relabeling a poor strategy as 'optimal' dramatically improves its evaluation. Additionally, LLM-generated solvers for the optimization problem scale poorly compared to purpose-built solvers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Attack graphs are a crucial tool for network vulnerability analysis, modeling potential attack paths that an adversary could exploit. Defense selection over attack graphs involves choosing security controls to minimize attacker success under budget constraints, often formulated as a game-theoretic optimization problem. Large language models are increasingly used in cybersecurity workflows, but their ability to perform structured reasoning versus relying on superficial cues remains unclear. This paper addresses that gap by systematically evaluating LLM performance on such tasks.

## [WA-JEPA: Rethinking Video JEPA for World-Action Modeling in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20974) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WA-JEPA introduces a novel adaptation of V-JEPA for autonomous driving, replacing random-mask completion with hybrid future-masked pretraining and deterministic regression with conditional flow matching. It achieves 91.7 EPDMS on NAVSIM-v2 and a best HD-Score of 0.4462 on the closed-loop HUGSIM benchmark without HUGSIM-specific fine-tuning. This work addresses fundamental limitations of V-JEPA for planning tasks, demonstrating that a V-JEPA-native world-action model can surpass existing end-to-end and world-action baselines. It offers a scalable paradigm for autonomous driving planning, potentially influencing future self-supervised learning approaches in robotics and embodied AI. WA-JEPA uses a joint future-action predictor that denoises future scene tokens and ego trajectories together in a unified spatiotemporal latent space. The model is pretrained on nuPlan videos and fine-tuned on NAVSIM, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Video Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (V-JEPA) is a self-supervised learning method that learns spatiotemporal representations by predicting latent features from masked video regions. However, its random-mask completion and deterministic regression are not suited for autonomous driving, which requires future-directed prediction coupled with action. World-action models (WAMs) are an emerging class of models that forecast future states to inform action decisions, and WA-JEPA fits into this trend by adapting V-JEPA for planning.

## [TracingFlow: Simulation-Free Trajectory Inference with Second-Order Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21070) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TracingFlow introduces a simulation-free Flow Matching framework that generalizes to second-order dynamics, providing an exact and efficient solution to the Dynamical Optimal Acceleration Transport (DOAT) problem. It regresses acceleration fields using neural networks to capture high-curvature transitions and nonlinear evolutions. This work addresses a key limitation of existing trajectory inference methods that rely on first-order dynamics, which often produce over-smoothed trajectories. By enabling second-order dynamics, TracingFlow can improve accuracy in generative modeling and single-cell omics, potentially leading to more biologically plausible reconstructions of cell differentiation processes. TracingFlow is evaluated on complex synthetic datasets and large-scale scRNA-seq datasets, achieving superior accuracy in distributional reconstruction and trajectory faithfulness. It also integrates lineage tracing priors to recover dynamical structures that are both mathematically optimal and biologically plausible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Trajectory inference aims to reconstruct continuous cellular transitions from sparse snapshots of single-cell omics data. Optimal Transport (OT) is a popular framework for this task, but most existing methods assume first-order dynamics, which ignore regulatory momentum and time-delayed responses. Flow Matching is a simulation-free generative modeling technique that learns velocity fields, and extending it to second-order dynamics allows modeling acceleration, capturing more complex behaviors.

## [Trustworthy RAG: Evaluation Agent Detects Misinformation and Knowledge Poisoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21095) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an evaluation agent for RAG systems that combines NLI-based factual verification, a five-signal poison detector, and a Trust Index to detect misinformation and knowledge poisoning before generation. On TruthfulQA with Llama 3.3 70B, it achieves 91% accuracy and 100% precision, with 100% recall on instruction injection. This addresses a critical security gap in RAG systems, where retrieved content is often trusted without verification, leaving them vulnerable to knowledge poisoning attacks. The proposed agent provides a practical middleware solution to enhance reliability and security, which is increasingly important as RAG is widely adopted in enterprise applications. The Trust Index is computed as T = 0.4F + 0.35C + 0.25(1-P) with a non-linear dampener for high-contamination contexts. The agent achieves a ROC-AUC of 0.73 to 0.81 across three LLMs, but in-place edits like entity swaps remain hard to detect, and cross-dataset generalization requires domain-specific calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) grounds LLM outputs in external knowledge to improve factuality, but it typically trusts retrieved content without verification. Knowledge poisoning is an attack where adversaries inject malicious documents into the knowledge source to cause targeted misinformation. Natural Language Inference (NLI) is a task that classifies the semantic relationship between a premise and hypothesis as entailment, contradiction, or neutral, which can be used for fact verification.

## [ClawSentry: Multi-Tier Security Monitor for Autonomous LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21101) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ClawSentry introduces a progressive multi-tier security supervision gateway for autonomous LLM agents, addressing risks across four loci of the agent control loop. It is open-source and framework-agnostic, supporting Codex, Claude Code, Kimi CLI, and Gemini CLI without modifying agent internals. This addresses a critical security gap in autonomous LLM agents, which can be hijacked to cause data exfiltration or privilege escalation. By providing a framework-agnostic solution, it has the potential to become a standard security layer for agent runtimes, significantly reducing attack success rates. ClawSentry uses a three-tier progressive decision engine: a deterministic L1 layer, a rule-anchored L2 semantic reviewer, and a read-only L3 evidence-seeking agent. On SkillInject with Codex/GPT-5.4, contextual ASR drops from 39.55% to 2.61%, while TSR remains stable at 83.05%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly moving from conversation to executing code and orchestrating external tools, introducing new security risks. Existing safeguards are typically local to one lifecycle boundary, but ClawSentry argues that agentic risk is progressive and can enter at four loci: skill admission, invocation-time intent, execution-time effect, and post-action consequence. The Agent Harness Protocol (AHP) abstraction allows a single policy to be applied across different agent frameworks.

## [Atom Learning Model Tokenizes Curriculum for Low-Cost Personalized Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21106) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The Atom Learning Model (ALM) tokenizes a school curriculum into 1,934 atomic learning units and 4,616 prerequisite links, automatically composing 6,648 questions for 373 students in two English secondary schools over seven weeks. The entire structure was built for between £615 and £1,230, with each composed question costing 26p. This approach offers a cost-effective, scalable method for personalized learning, potentially reducing the need for human-authored questions and difficulty calibration. It could democratize adaptive educational technology, especially in resource-constrained settings, by making question generation and ability assessment nearly automatic. The system uses a single graph structure where questions are sets of atoms plus prerequisites, and student ability is a score per atom, requiring no difficulty parameter fitting. Notably, the composer's difficulty label had a rank correlation of -0.0123 with measured facility, indicating that language models cannot judge question difficulty, and the deployment never served questions deeper than two prerequisite steps, leaving the central premise unfalsified.

**Background**: Tokenization in AI typically refers to breaking text into subword units, but here it is applied to curriculum content, decomposing it into atomic learning units. The approach is validated in real classrooms, contrasting with many AI education studies that rely on synthetic data.

## [CIVA: Efficient Value-Subspace Attacks on Visual World-Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21114) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CIVA, a novel white-box attack that exploits a low-dimensional value-subspace induced by the victim's critic to efficiently and coherently perturb visual world-model agents like DreamerV3. It achieves the largest reward drop of 26.07% on DMC walker walk while maintaining low temporal variation. This work addresses a critical gap in adversarial robustness for world-model-based agents, which are increasingly used in reinforcement learning. By demonstrating a practical and efficient attack, it highlights vulnerabilities that need to be addressed for safe deployment in real-world applications. CIVA operates in two stages: offline, it probes the frozen victim with critic-guided PGD and extracts a low-rank value-subspace via SVD; online, it optimizes only subspace coefficients, smoothed with EMA, and maps them back to pixels. Experiments on DMC walker walk, Atari Pong, and Crafter show consistent outperformance over five recent methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Visual world-model agents, such as DreamerV3, operate through a recurrent latent state rather than a single observation, which weakens frame-wise attacks. Adversarial attacks on reinforcement learning agents are a growing concern, with methods like PGD being common. CIVA leverages the critic's value function to find a low-dimensional subspace that captures value-sensitive dynamics, enabling efficient and temporally coherent attacks.

The paper introduces PUN (Plausible Unknown Names), a protocol for constructing and validating person names that are operationally unknown, combining Wikidata-derived components, web-enabled LLM screening, and controlled search revalidation. The protocol defines operational unknownness as having a plausible First-Last form, no indexed full-name evidence, and no ambiguity signals under a documented validation run. The human study found that accepted names are more name-like than controls, while participants recovered person evidence in only 3% of cases.

## [Agent Memory Poisoning Defeats Content Screening and Provenance Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21230) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that poisoning just 1.2% of a LongMemEval corpus drops accuracy from 0.850 to 0.300, and that a four-stage content screening pipeline with 0.832 recall on indirect prompt injection rejects none of 360 poisoned memories. The authors also show that provenance-weighted retrieval is statistically indistinguishable from no defense (p=0.80) and argue for bounded occupancy constraints at retrieval. This research exposes critical vulnerabilities in AI agent memory systems, showing that content-only screening is fundamentally insufficient because distinguishing false from true assertions requires external grounding. The findings challenge existing defense mechanisms and highlight the urgent need for more robust memory security, impacting the safety and reliability of AI agents in real-world applications. The paper uses plainly worded false assertions generated in a single pass with no instruction, trigger, or retriever optimization. In a mixed-provenance corpus, a stronger provenance weight recovers utility only by excluding untrusted content, but when the answer-bearing evidence itself is untrusted, evidence recall drops to zero and accuracy to 0.0417.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Agent memory poisoning is an attack where hostile text is written into an AI agent's long-term memory, so later sessions retrieve it as trusted facts. LongMemEval is a benchmark for testing long-term memory of chat assistants. Content screening and provenance-weighted retrieval are common defenses, but this paper shows their limitations.

## [EnSI-RAG: Entity-Structure-Indexed RAG for Long-Document QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21252) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EnSI-RAG introduces a query-independent, entity-centered indexing scheme for retrieval-augmented generation, where each record (e, t, k, v) captures an entity, its type, semantic category, and value, while retaining links to source passages. On the Loong and Oolong benchmarks, it achieves an average accuracy of 78.24, which is 6.62 points higher than the published baseline scores. This approach addresses a key limitation of traditional RAG methods that index raw chunks, which often fail when evidence spans multiple entities or requires multi-hop reasoning. By separating evidence localization from answer synthesis, EnSI-RAG could improve retrieval accuracy and traceability in long-document QA, benefiting applications like legal, medical, and scientific research. The index is built at write time, making it query-independent, and each record retains links to original source passages for traceability. The framework uses an LLM to synthesize retrieved passages into the final answer, and the code is available on GitHub.

Traditional RAG methods typically split documents into chunks and retrieve them via embedding similarity, which can fail when chunk boundaries separate entities from supporting evidence or when questions require multi-hop reasoning across the corpus. EnSI-RAG proposes an entity-centered index to overcome these limitations.

A new paper introduces a primal-only accelerated Newton method for convex functions with Lipschitz continuous Hessian, achieving a global convergence rate of O(1/k^3) in functional residual. The method requires only one linear solve per iteration, without auxiliary subproblems or dual corrections. This is the first second-order method to achieve O(1/k^3) convergence with only one linear solve per iteration, potentially making accelerated Newton methods more practical for large-scale optimization. It could influence future research in convex optimization and inspire new algorithms for composite and Bregman settings. The method uses a simple predetermined parameter choice and can be implemented in a Hessian-free manner using an inexact linear solver while preserving the fast global rate. It extends to arbitrary geometry via Bregman divergence and to composite optimization problems.

However, single-agent TTS faces scalability bottlenecks, such as increased inference costs and stability issues from long reasoning traces. Multi-agent systems (MAS) offer a way to decompose reasoning into manageable contexts across multiple agents, potentially mitigating these issues. The M500 dataset and fine-tuned models demonstrate this approach's effectiveness.

## [SEISMO: LLM Agent Boosts Sample-Efficient Molecular Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00663) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SEISMO is a new LLM agent for inference-time molecular optimization that conditions each proposal on natural-language task descriptions, the full optimization trajectory, and machine-readable explainability feedback, improving sample efficiency over existing optimizers and zero-shot LLM generation across drug-discovery-relevant tasks. This addresses a critical bottleneck in drug discovery where molecular property evaluation relies on costly and rate-limited oracles, making sample efficiency crucial. By leveraging explainability and trajectory information, SEISMO could accelerate the discovery of new drugs and keep medicinal chemists central to the optimization process. SEISMO uses post-hoc explainability methods and sub-score decompositions to generate machine-readable feedback, and its gains grow as explanatory feedback is enriched. The agent is built with LangGraph and supports custom scoring functions and optimization targets, allowing medicinal chemists to inspect reasoning and intervene via natural language.

## [LLM Introspection Claims Questioned by New Critique](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26242) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper argues that recent evidence for LLM introspection is premature, proposing two necessary conditions (privileged access and second-order computation) and re-examining two paradigms that previously claimed to demonstrate introspection. The authors find that these paradigms fail to meet the conditions, concluding that current evidence is insufficient to establish metacognitive monitoring in LLMs. This critique is significant because it challenges a growing body of research on LLM introspection, which has implications for AI transparency and oversight. By establishing clear criteria for what counts as introspection, the paper could influence future experimental designs and prevent overinterpretation of model behaviors. The paper identifies two conditions for introspection: privileged access (not solvable from input cues) and second-order computation (meta-representations of first-order representations). Re-examining two paradigms, they find that classifiers using only input match in-context predictions, and models cannot reliably distinguish internal tampering from input manipulation, suggesting generic anomaly detection rather than true introspection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Introspection in LLMs refers to the ability of models to detect and report their own internal states, a topic of growing interest due to implications for transparency and oversight. Human metacognition research provides a framework for studying such abilities, emphasizing privileged access and second-order representations. Recent studies have claimed LLMs can introspect, but this paper argues those claims are premature.

<a id="item-83"></a>
## [GRASP: Gated Regression-Aware Skill Proposer Boosts LLM Agent Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29668) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GRASP introduces a gated regression-aware skill proposer that treats LLM agent improvement as a sequence of edits to a bounded skill library, admitting only candidates that yield net improvement on a balanced held-out probe under a hard regression budget. On MedAgentBench, it lifts gpt-oss-120b from 40.6% to 88.8%, exceeding the strongest baseline by 21.0 points and improving every other base model by 17.2 to 40.3 points. This work addresses a critical flaw in prior self-improvement methods that can silently regress previously correct behaviors, which is essential for reliable deployment of LLM agents in structured environments like clinical settings. The approach is generalizable across multiple base models and benchmarks, potentially improving trust and safety in automated decision-making systems. The gains are attributed to comparative proposal generation, the acceptance gate, and the hard regression budget, not to skill writing itself, which without validation is no better than using no skills. The mechanism works in non-clinical environments with verifiable structure but is flat where the action space is open-ended; frozen libraries transfer across models and benchmarks sharing a tool-calling convention but degrade under interface mismatch.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents operating in structured environments often fail in operational ways, and their reliability depends on procedural knowledge of the environment. Prior self-improvement methods accumulate natural-language guidance without checking that each new item preserves previously correct behavior, leading to silent regressions. GRASP addresses this by using a gated regression-aware skill proposer that validates each skill edit against a balanced held-out probe. The evaluation uses FHIR-based clinical benchmarks like MedAgentBench, which score procedural reliability against FHIR state rather than clinical correctness.

## [Weak Critics Improve Strong Models via On-Policy Critique Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00424) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces weak-critic strong oversight, using weak models as critics rather than labelers, and proposes progressive on-policy critique distillation (OPCD) to improve strong models on reasoning and alignment benchmarks. This addresses a key challenge in scalable oversight: when weak supervisors cannot provide reliable labels, weak critiques can still guide strong models. The method offers a practical path for aligning increasingly capable AI systems with limited human oversight. The paper shows that critique quality is crucial for improving frozen strong models at inference time. OPCD filters high-quality critiques and distills critic-guided behavior into the strong model using adaptive self-teacher signals, improving performance over training epochs.

**Background**: Scalable oversight is the process by which weaker AI systems supervise stronger ones, a key strategy for controlling superintelligent AI. Weak-to-strong generalization explores how strong models can learn from weak supervisors. Critique distillation is an emerging paradigm that uses selective critiques to enhance model precision and robustness.

## [Categorical Framework for Self-Revising AI Discovery Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01444) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a category-theoretic framework for agentic scientific discovery, distinguishing retrieval, search, and discovery via functorial transport and regime transitions. It instantiates the framework in two systems: Builder/Breaker and CategoryScienceClaw. This framework provides a rigorous mathematical treatment of discovery as regime transitions, potentially impacting AI-driven science by offering a formal specification for self-revising systems. It could help standardize how AI systems are evaluated for genuine discovery versus mere retrieval. In a fixed regime, system state is a copresheaf, and discovery is a verified regime transition using left Kan extension to preserve old artifacts. The framework is instantiated in materials science, with examples including a protein-mechanics model and a fiber-network example using an AIC gate.

**Background**: Category theory is a branch of mathematics that deals with abstract structures and relationships, using concepts like functors and natural transformations. Left Kan extensions are a universal construction that allows extending a functor along another, often used to transport structures between categories. In this context, the framework models scientific discovery as a change in the representational regime, where old artifacts are transported to the new regime via functorial methods.

## [Quantization Alters LLM Behavior Despite Preserved Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08734) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Correctness Agreement, a decision-level metric that measures the intersection of correct predictions between a base LLM and its quantized variant. Using this metric across multiple models and bit levels (8-bit to 2-bit), the authors find that quantization causes behavioral shifts even when accuracy and perplexity are preserved. This work challenges the common practice of evaluating quantization methods solely with accuracy and perplexity, which can mask significant behavioral changes. It provides a new evaluation dimension that could improve model compression and deployment practices, ensuring quantized models behave more predictably in real-world applications. The study reveals a breakpoint at low bit widths and shows that query and key projections are more sensitive to quantization than value and output projections. The findings highlight the 'illusion of equivalency' between base and quantized models, emphasizing the need for behavioral evaluation beyond traditional metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) is a widely used technique to compress large language models (LLMs) by reducing the precision of weights and activations, making them deployable on resource-constrained devices. Traditionally, the effectiveness of quantization is measured by accuracy and perplexity, which assess overall performance but may not capture subtle changes in model behavior. The proposed Correctness Agreement metric aims to fill this gap by focusing on decision-level consistency between the original and quantized models.

## [PRISM Probe Detects Physical Jailbreak in LLM Planners via Hidden States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15218) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces PRISM, a single-layer L2-regularized logistic probe that analyzes hidden-state risk space to detect physical jailbreak in LLM planners, achieving 86.2-87.7% accuracy on SafeAgentBench with 11.7-13.7% false-positive rates, outperforming LLM judges. It also introduces an interaction-balanced revision of PhysicalJailbreakBench-2K to control for lexical shortcuts. This research addresses a critical safety gap in embodied AI, where linguistically benign instructions can become dangerous when executed in the physical world. By showing that physical jailbreak is separable from textual jailbreak in hidden states and providing an efficient probe, it offers a promising direction for more reliable safety mechanisms in LLM-based planners. PRISM uses full hidden states from LLMs like Qwen2.5-3B/7B/14B/32B, Phi-3.5, and SmolLM2, and achieves 0.718 AUC on the revised PJB-2K benchmark, compared to 0.398 for a physics-free label control. The method avoids reliance on shortcut-prone paired templates and shows that word-TFIDF and embedding layers remain at chance (AUC 0.497 and 0.500) on the underlying 10,000-row pool.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used as high-level planners for embodied agents, which execute instructions in physical or simulated environments. Textual jailbreak refers to prompts that bypass safety alignment to elicit harmful text, while physical jailbreak involves instructions that appear safe linguistically but lead to unsafe actions when grounded in the physical world. Hidden-state analysis examines the internal representations of LLMs to detect safety-relevant signals that may not be apparent in the output text.

## [Formal Model Shows Fragility of Value Under Imperfect AI Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28881) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper presents a formal model of the AI alignment problem, identifying conditions under which an agent with an imperfect proxy value function, called an η-catastrophic value function, would be deployed and cause catastrophic outcomes in the limit of optimization power. This work provides rigorous theoretical grounding for the long-standing concern that overoptimizing an imperfect proxy for human values can lead to catastrophic outcomes. It underscores the importance of designing AI systems that limit optimization pressure, such as quantilizers, rather than relying solely on pre-deployment alignment training. The model assumes an agent undergoes idealized alignment training that guarantees its value function satisfies a proxy condition before optimizing the world. The primary results identify conditions on the human value function and the accuracy of proxy conditions under which an η-catastrophic agent would be deployed, highlighting the danger of overoptimization.

This paper introduces a method for reasoning language models to learn adaptive reasoning effort allocation by selecting among NoThink, Short, and Long modes via GRPO, improving efficiency and accuracy. The approach allows tuning prices for diverse objectives like sustainability, fairness, and welfare, and demonstrates significant improvements in resource sustainability over market equilibrium in scarce environments. This work bridges AI and economics by offering a practical, learning-based method to correct market failures caused by externalities, which traditional competitive markets ignore. The policymaker operates in an environment with other learning agents, using deep reinforcement learning to adjust prices. The paper highlights that the policymaker significantly outperforms market equilibrium in maintaining resource sustainability, especially in scarce resource environments, while also allowing trade-offs among objectives like fairness and wastefulness.

Traditional competitive markets fail to account for these, leading to resource misallocation and unsustainable outcomes. Common-pool resources, like fisheries or forests, are particularly vulnerable, and sustainability has become a key concern. ## [CulTrace: Tracing Internal Cultural Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08879) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CulTrace is a novel mechanistic interpretability method that probes the internal representations of large language models (LLMs) to trace how cultural knowledge is processed across layers and integrated during cultural question answering. It reveals a consistent staged trajectory of cultural reasoning and demonstrates imbalances, including delayed culture resolution and more confusion with less-represented cultures. This work addresses a critical gap in understanding how LLMs internally represent cultural knowledge, moving beyond output-based evaluations to uncover why models generate incorrect responses. It provides insights that could improve cultural awareness in AI systems, which is increasingly important as LLMs are deployed across diverse cultural contexts. CulTrace identifies a three-stage reasoning trajectory: models first engage with the question's domain, then resolve the relevant culture, and finally narrow in on an answer. The method also reveals that cultural reasoning is imbalanced, with delayed culture resolution and increased confusion for less-represented cultures, highlighting potential biases in model internals.

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability is a subfield of explainable AI that aims to reverse-engineer neural networks by analyzing their internal structures and circuits, similar to understanding conventional software. Prior work on cultural awareness in LLMs typically evaluated outputs, but CulTrace uses probing of internal representations to understand how cultural knowledge is stored and processed within the model parameters.

## [AIRL-S: Unifying RL and Search-Based Test-Time Scaling via Adversarial Inverse RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14313) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AIRL-S introduces a unified framework that combines Adversarial Inverse Reinforcement Learning (AIRL) with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), learning a dense step-wise reward model directly from reference trajectories without labeled process data. This learned reward model serves both as a training signal and as a verifier for search-based test-time scaling, improving average performance by 9% over the base model across eight benchmarks in math, science, and code generation. This work addresses key limitations of existing test-time scaling methods: outcome-based RL suffers from training instability and sample inefficiency, while static Process Reward Models (PRMs) require expensive step-wise supervision and are prone to reward hacking. By unifying RL and search-based scaling with a learned PRM, AIRL-S offers a more robust and cost-effective approach, potentially improving LLM reasoning capabilities across complex tasks. The framework uses AIRL to infer a dense step-wise reward model from reference trajectories, eliminating the need for labeled process data. The learned PRM is used both as a training signal for GRPO and as a verifier for search-based test-time scaling, and the paper analyzes how AIRL and GRPO objectives complement each other and how the PRM transfers across generators and search algorithms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling strategies for LLMs typically rely on either reinforcement learning with sparse outcome rewards or search-based methods guided by static Process Reward Models (PRMs). Outcome-based RL often suffers from training instability and sample inefficiency, while static PRMs require expensive step-wise supervision and are susceptible to reward hacking due to distributional shifts. AIRL is a method to learn reward functions from expert demonstrations via adversarial training, and GRPO is a policy optimization algorithm that computes rewards through groupwise comparisons, eliminating the need for a separate critic model.

## [AgentOCR: Visual Token Compression for LLM Agent History](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04786) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgentOCR, a new framework from arXiv, represents LLM agent history as compact rendered images instead of text, using segment optical caching and compression-aware reinforcement learning to reduce token and memory costs. Experiments on ALFWorld and search-based QA show it preserves over 95% of text-based agent performance while cutting token consumption by more than 50%. This addresses a critical bottleneck in multi-turn LLM agents, where growing textual histories inflate token and memory costs, limiting practical deployment. By leveraging visual tokens' higher information density, AgentOCR could enable more efficient and scalable agentic systems, impacting applications like automated reasoning and tool use. The framework introduces segment optical caching, which decomposes history into hashable segments and maintains a visual cache to eliminate redundant re-rendering, achieving a 20x rendering speedup. It also employs agentic self-compression, where the agent emits a compression rate and is trained with a compression-aware reward to balance task success and token efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as agents that interact with environments over multiple turns, but their textual histories grow rapidly, inflating token and memory costs. Visual tokens have higher information density than text, so representing history as images could reduce costs. Reinforcement learning (RL) is used to train agents to optimize task success, and AgentOCR extends this with a compression-aware reward to encourage token efficiency.

## [GRALIS Unifies Coalition and Gradient XAI with Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05480) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GRALIS (Gradient-Riesz Averaged Locally-Integrated Shapley) is introduced as a unified estimator that fuses Shapley coalition weights and locality kernels with an Integrated-Gradients-style conditioned path, providing an exact closed-form completeness deficit and a finite-sample bound of O(1/sqrt(m)) + O(1/k^2). This work addresses a known gap in XAI by unifying coalition-based and gradient-based methods, which are often complementary but not directly comparable. It offers theoretical guarantees that neither mechanism provides alone, potentially improving the reliability and comparability of post-hoc explanations in deep learning. The framework is underpinned by a representation-theoretic result: every additive, linear, continuous attribution functional has a unique canonical representation via the Riesz Representation Theorem, proved componentwise. This class includes SHAP, IG, and LIME, but excludes nonlinear functionals like standard GradCAM or attention maps.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Post-hoc XAI methods for deep networks, such as GradCAM, SHAP, LIME, and Integrated Gradients, originate from different theoretical foundations and are hard to compare. A recent benchmark found coalition-based and gradient-based methods complementary, with method selection as the only remedy. GRALIS fuses these mechanisms into a single estimator with certified guarantees, and includes seven theorems establishing correspondences with Shapley Interaction Values and Hoeffding/Sobol decompositions.

## [Behavior-Consistent RL Reduces Cross-Run Divergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21214) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper formalizes behavior-consistent reinforcement learning, proposing Q-value Expectile Disagreement (QED), a state-dependent temperature schedule that uses double-critic disagreement as a proxy for cross-run disagreement. Empirically, QED reduces across-run divergence by two orders of magnitude across 18 continuous-control tasks without sacrificing performance. This work addresses the critical issue of high variance across RL training runs, which undermines reliability in real-world deployments. By providing theoretical bounds and a practical method, it could significantly improve the trustworthiness of RL systems in safety-critical applications. The paper proves that for Boltzmann policies, setting temperature proportional to Q-function disagreement bounds pairwise KL divergence between induced policies. However, naively increasing entropy can impair optimization and amplify off-policy error, motivating the QED method which uses double-critic disagreement as a single-run proxy.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) trains agents to maximize cumulative reward, but often exhibits high variance across training runs, leading to unreliable performance. The paper introduces the Word Coverage Score (WCS), a new metric that quantifies how standard sampling filters (Top-p, Top-k, Min-p) prune low-frequency human words during LLM decoding. It provides quantitative evidence that these filters act as unintended censorship, reducing lexical diversity. This work highlights a previously overlooked factor in repetitive text generation—decoding mechanics rather than just model knowledge or training data. It could influence how developers tune sampling parameters to balance coherence and lexical richness, benefiting NLP research and applications. The WCS measures the lexical survival rate of low-frequency, high-information words as a function of sampling parameters, auditing open-weight models on human-authored corpus fragments. The study uses the PG-19 dataset to evaluate lexical reachability in long-context environments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models generate text by sampling from a probability distribution over tokens. Sampling filters like Top-p, Top-k, and Min-p truncate the distribution to focus on likely tokens, which can inadvertently exclude rare but contextually appropriate words. The WCS provides a diagnostic tool to quantify this exclusion, helping researchers understand the trade-off between coherence and diversity.

## [Audio Interaction Model: Always-On Streaming Audio AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05121) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper formalizes the Audio Interaction Model paradigm, an always-on perceive-decide-respond loop for streaming audio, and introduces the Audio-Interaction model with the SoundFlow system. It also presents StreamAudio-2M, a 2.6M-item, 302k-hour dataset, and Proactive-Sound-Bench, demonstrating competitive performance across 8 benchmarks. This work shifts audio AI from offline, task-specific models to a unified, always-on streaming paradigm, enabling proactive intervention and continuous listening. It could impact applications like real-time assistants, hearing aids, and ambient intelligence, and provides a large-scale dataset and benchmark to drive further research. The Audio-Interaction model uses dual-loss training and asynchronous FIFO inference, with comprehension-aware silence/response supervision. StreamAudio-2M covers 7 capability families and 28 sub-tasks, and the model remains competitive on mainstream tasks while enabling spoken-instruction robustness, long-stream interaction, and proactive intervention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large Audio Language Models (LALMs) typically operate offline, processing complete audio clips, while streaming systems are often specialized for tasks like ASR or dialogue. This paper proposes a unified paradigm that continuously listens, decides when to respond, and does so without stopping, addressing the interactive nature of real-world audio.

## [Task Exchangeability Enables Valid Inference with Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13629) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a new statistical condition called task exchangeability, which allows researchers to use synthetic data in scientific research with provable validity guarantees. The framework is demonstrated on public opinion surveys with silicon samples and AI evaluation with autoraters. As synthetic data from LLMs and generative models becomes increasingly common in research, this work addresses a critical concern about bias and validity. It provides a principled way to ensure that conclusions drawn from synthetic data are statistically sound, potentially accelerating discovery while maintaining scientific integrity. The key insight is that researchers must identify historical tasks with real data that are exchangeable with the current task in a formal statistical sense. The paper also provides extensions that offer guarantees beyond exchangeability, broadening the applicability of the framework.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Synthetic data, such as LLM-generated 'silicon samples' or generative model outputs, is increasingly used in scientific research to accelerate discovery. However, synthetic data can be biased, noisy, or misspecified, raising concerns about the validity of inferences. Task exchangeability is a formal condition that ensures the synthetic data generation process is statistically aligned with the real data across tasks, enabling valid inference.

## [Metanym Game: A Ground-Truth-Free LLM Benchmark That Rises with the Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21008) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Metanym Game, a novel LLM benchmark where models generate and rate analogous statements, replacing ground truth with the SVD of the rating matrix. It demonstrates a high correlation (Pearson r = 0.97) with GPQA Diamond, suggesting analogy is central to LLM intelligence. This benchmark offers a scalable, ground-truth-free evaluation method that can adapt to improving models, potentially serving as a steering signal for self-improving AI. Its strong correlation with established benchmarks like GPQA Diamond validates its practical relevance and could reshape how LLMs are evaluated. The benchmark uses peer ratings and SVD to score players as both generators and judges, with judges weighted by rating consistency for subjective criteria. The best generators turn out to be middling judges, and a council of the five best issues official ratings with contestable seats for scalability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional LLM benchmarks rely on fixed ground truth, such as human-written questions, which can become outdated as models improve. The Metanym Game eliminates this by having models generate and rate content in a self-contained game, using SVD to derive a consensus. This approach interweaves multiple constructs of intelligence, allowing both composite and reductionistic analysis.

## [MatMMExtract: Open-Source Pipeline for Panel-Level Image-Text Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29667) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MatMMExtract is an open-source pipeline that decomposes compound figures in materials science papers into sub-panels and generates grounded annotations, producing a large dataset of 391,606 panel-level image-text pairs from 180,571 figures across 14,810 open-access articles. This addresses a critical bottleneck in AI-driven scientific knowledge extraction by unlocking the visual record in materials science literature. The scale and quality of the dataset (MatSciFig) and the introduction of MaterialScope for panel detection have broad implications for materials informatics and multimodal AI research. The pipeline uses a fine-tuned YOLO12-m detector on MaterialScope, achieving mAP_50 of 0.9227. Among six language models, Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite provided the best cost-quality trade-off, with 82% outputs rated good and a hallucination rate of 4.8%. A dual-encoder retrieval baseline on MatSciFig achieved a 4.4x improvement in R@1 over zero-shot CLIP.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Materials science literature contains decades of experimental knowledge in figures, but compound figures (multiple sub-panels under one caption) make direct image-text pairing unreliable. MatMMExtract decomposes these figures and uses a large language model guided by a materials science taxonomy to generate structured annotations, enabling large-scale multimodal dataset creation.

<a id="item-115"></a>
## [Three Questions to Understand What World Models Represent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06640) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a framework of three questions—reachability, admission, and assignment—to analyze what information world models represent and how it is acquired through different training routes. It provides empirical tests in environments with known required coordinates, showing that a direction cannot enter the latent unless some training signal can identify it, and that the number of independent predictions determines how many coordinates are installed. This work addresses a fundamental question in representation learning and reinforcement learning: how to interpret what a world model has learned. By distinguishing between reachability, admission, and assignment, it offers a more nuanced understanding of latent representations, which could improve model interpretability and guide the design of training objectives. The paper tests the framework in environments with a known set of required coordinates, showing that reconstruction, recurrence, or filtering may already recover some coordinates, leaving no residual direction for a reward or value head to admit. It also demonstrates that reachability is not admission: a temporal second-moment coefficient can remain absent under next-token prediction when accumulating it is a fraction of a percent of the loss, but a head that predicts the coefficient restores it. Additionally, assignment is a different test, as two routes that each carry the same variable when trained alone do not swap the carrier when the cost of removal is reversed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: World models are neural networks that learn compressed representations of an environment's dynamics, often used in reinforcement learning to enable efficient planning and policy learning. They can be trained through various routes such as observation reconstruction, recurrent state, temporal filtering, and explicit task supervision. Understanding which information is captured in the latent space and how it is acquired is crucial for improving model interpretability and performance.

## [New Robustness Margin CRoMa Improves Pathology Foundation Model Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25497) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CRoMa (Cross-confounder Robustness Margin), a signed, per-sample robustness metric for pathology foundation models. It addresses structural limitations of the existing Robustness Index (RI), enabling reliable cross-model comparison and distribution-level robustness analysis. CRoMa provides a more principled way to evaluate robustness in medical AI, which is critical as pathology foundation models approach clinical deployment. It allows models to be compared on the same cohort and robustness to be analyzed as a distribution, potentially improving model selection and generalization assessment. CRoMa was evaluated across 20 tile-level encoders on three benchmarks, with median CRoMa rankings highly consistent (Spearman rho ~0.90). However, every encoder retained confounder-dominated samples, and higher median CRoMa correlated with smaller shortcut-induced performance losses in downstream linear probes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Pathology foundation models encode non-biological variation from tissue preparation, staining, and scanning, leading to shortcut learning that harms generalization across institutions. The Robustness Index (RI) was proposed to assess whether local representation geometry is dominated by biological or non-biological variation, but its construction has limitations. CRoMa measures whether samples sharing the same biology but different confounders lie closer than samples sharing the same confounder but different biology, providing a per-sample margin.

MobileForge is introduced as the first benchmark for project-level multi-screen mobile app generation, featuring real apps, human-reviewed screens, page-relationship annotations, and navigation test specifications. It evaluates five axes: build, navigation, visual fidelity, maintainability, and efficiency, and proposes state-isolated navigation testing and anchor-referenced list-wise visual evaluation. This benchmark addresses critical gaps in existing design-to-code benchmarks, which focus on single-page generation and cannot evaluate cross-page navigation or project-wide maintainability. It provides a standardized way to assess multimodal LLMs in real-world mobile app development, potentially driving improvements in AI-assisted software engineering. The benchmark includes real mobile apps with human-reviewed screens and structured page-relationship annotations. State-isolated navigation testing prevents cascading failures, and anchor-referenced list-wise visual evaluation improves visual-judge reliability. Evaluations on six frontier multimodal LLMs show that current models can compile and reach correct pages, but interactive navigation remains unreliable, and visual fidelity and maintainability lag.

## [SparkleDock: GPU-Accelerated Flexible Docking with 18.9x Speedup](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07078) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SparkleDock, a scalable GSO-based docking framework, achieves near-real-time flexible macromolecular docking on GPU supercomputers by redesigning the GSO algorithm and reformulating energy scoring for Tensor Cores. It reports 9.7x and 18.9x speedups over LightDock on single A100 and H100 GPUs, respectively, and over two orders of magnitude acceleration at scale. This breakthrough significantly reduces the computational cost of flexible docking, enabling large-scale virtual screening that was previously impractical. It could accelerate drug discovery and biomolecular interaction studies, benefiting computational biology and pharmaceutical research. SparkleDock introduces performance-model-driven scheduling for load balancing and out-of-core scaling across GPUs. On 512 GPUs, it reduces docking time from hours to seconds, demonstrating substantial scalability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Flexible macromolecular docking models the three-dimensional structure of complexes formed by interacting biomolecules, allowing internal geometry changes. LightDock uses Glowworm Swarm Optimization (GSO) for accuracy but suffers from limited parallelism and load imbalance on GPUs. Tensor Cores are specialized GPU cores that accelerate matrix operations, which SparkleDock leverages for efficient energy scoring.

## [Hamilton-Zero: Neural Tensor-Network Foundation Model for Quantum Ground States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11911) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Hamilton-Zero, a 0.5B parameter neural tensor-network foundation model that learns ground states of arbitrary quadratic qubit Hamiltonians via manifold variational optimization. The model is pre-trained on hundreds of thousands of Hamiltonian systems and can generalize to systems up to 8100 qubits. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in quantum computing by amortizing ground-state calculations across many Hamiltonians, potentially enabling faster simulations beyond classical methods. It combines techniques from large language models and deep reinforcement learning, marking a novel intersection of AI and quantum many-body physics. The model uses manifold variational optimization over centrally odd scalar functions on SU(2)^N, with Hamiltonian acting via Lie derivatives and custom automatic differentiation primitives. It employs a novel SU(2) replica-exchange Langevin sampler and sharded natural-gradient optimization with an extension of KFAC, trained on systems up to 64 qubits and fine-tuned up to 1024 qubits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum ground-state problems are central to quantum chemistry and materials science, but classical simulation becomes intractable for large systems. Neural quantum states (NQS) use neural networks to represent wavefunctions, but traditional approaches optimize from scratch for each Hamiltonian. Foundation models, pre-trained on diverse tasks, offer a way to amortize this cost across many systems, as demonstrated by Hamilton-Zero.

## [Natural-Gradient Message Passing Bridges Information Geometry and Variational Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15922) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces natural-gradient message passing (NGMP), a new message passing scheme on Forney-style factor graphs. It shows that the natural-gradient stationary condition of variational inference has an edge-local form, where each edge's natural parameter equals the sum of two projected messages from incident factors. NGMP offers a novel theoretical connection between information geometry and message passing, potentially improving uncertainty calibration in probabilistic models. It provides a more accurate alternative to variational message passing when uncertainty persists, with applications in time series forecasting and regression. NGMP preserves the representable part of exact messages, unlike variational message passing which averages the factor under neighboring beliefs. Experiments on Poisson smoothing, heteroskedastic regression, and hourly ETTh forecasting show gains mainly in uncertainty calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Message passing algorithms like belief propagation operate on factor graphs to compute marginals. Variational inference approximates intractable posteriors by optimizing a free energy, and the Bethe free energy provides a framework for approximate inference. Natural gradients are a second-order optimization method that respects the geometry of the parameter space.

<a id="item-125"></a>
## [SuTRA: Morphology-Aware Tokenization Boosts Indic NLP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18087) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SuTRA introduces a morphology-aware tokenization algorithm that preserves akshara units and penalizes merges crossing morphological boundaries, reducing morphological shattering. It achieves peak gains of +14.7% in Boundary F1 and +34% in semantic recoverability over BPE, with an average +8.08 chrF2 improvement in machine translation. This addresses a known limitation of frequency-based tokenizers for morphologically rich Indic languages, potentially improving downstream tasks like machine translation and sentiment analysis. It also releases a new morphological segmentation dataset for Hindi, Marathi, and Gujarati, aiding future research. SuTRA preserves akshara indivisibility and uses a penalty for merges crossing morphological boundaries. The new dataset covers Hindi, Marathi, and Gujarati, and the method shows significant gains over BPE, though its impact is niche and not yet widely validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Subword tokenizers like BPE optimize statistical compression but ignore morphological structure, which is problematic for morphologically rich languages where basic units are aksharas (complex orthographic syllables). This over-fragmentation, termed morphological shattering, splits roots and affixes arbitrarily, harming downstream performance. SuTRA aims to fix this by being morphology-aware.

## [CoToGrasp: Contact-Topology-Conditioned Dexterous Grasp Synthesis via Canonical Workspace Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19776) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CoToGrasp introduces a generative framework that synthesizes diverse, stable dexterous grasps strictly conditioned on specific contact topologies, trained entirely in an object-agnostic manner using a feature-based canonical workspace. It achieves zero-shot generalization to unseen objects and outperforms existing taxonomy-guided planners on the DexGraspNet dataset, with physical validation on a real robot. This work addresses a critical limitation in dexterous grasp planning by conditioning synthesis on functional contact topologies rather than just physical stability, enabling task-aware grasping without expensive object-annotated datasets. The object-agnostic training and zero-shot generalization could significantly advance robotics manipulation and reduce the need for per-object data collection. The method introduces a feature-based canonical workspace that projects local object features into a unified gripper-centric domain, decoupling semantic functional intent from arbitrary object geometry. Extensive evaluations on DexGraspNet show state-of-the-art performance, and physical viability is demonstrated on a real robot platform; code is available on the project website.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Dexterous grasp planning traditionally focuses on physical stability, often ignoring how the grasp should be performed to support downstream tasks. Conditioning on human grasp taxonomies typically requires large, object-annotated datasets, which are costly to obtain. CoToGrasp bypasses this by learning an object-agnostic contact manifold in a canonical workspace, allowing zero-shot generalization to new objects. This approach is relevant to robotics and manipulation research, where task-aware grasping is increasingly important.

<a id="item-127"></a>
## [CJSD: Exact Decomposition of Dataset Shift into Covariate and Functional Components](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19885) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the Conditional Jensen-Shannon Discrepancy (CJSD), a metric that exactly decomposes dataset shift into covariate and functional components using two discriminators. It proves several properties and demonstrates practical estimation via two classifiers, achieving AUC 1.0 on a battery of 202 dataset pairs. This work addresses a fundamental problem in domain adaptation and dataset comparison by providing an exact decomposition of shift types, which is crucial for model robustness and fairness audits. The approach avoids complex generative models, making it accessible and broadly applicable across machine learning applications. The CJSD is defined as D_CJS = CE(Z|X) - CE(Z|X,Y), which equals the conditional mutual information I(Z;Y|X). It satisfies a covariate-null property, a drift-mass law, a one-sided misspecification-control inequality, and a fixed-measure metrization. Empirically, the discriminator-based estimator remains effective up to d=256, while a kNN plug-in fails from d=64.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Dataset shift occurs when the distribution of data changes between training and deployment, often categorized into covariate shift (changes in input distribution) and concept drift (changes in the relationship between inputs and labels). Traditional methods often conflate these two types, making it difficult to diagnose and address model degradation. The chain rule of mutual information provides a principled way to decompose total shift into components, which the CJSD leverages.

PrimeAgentOrchestrator (PAO) is a new system that spawns Claude Code instances pre-loaded with relevant memories from personal databases, addressing the empty-context problem in LLM coding agents. It queries two memory backends in parallel and injects the compiled briefing via filesystem injection, with deployment reported over four months (December 2025–March 2026). This addresses a known limitation of LLM coding agents—lack of persistent memory across sessions—which can significantly improve developer productivity and continuity in long-term projects. It also demonstrates a practical approach to integrating heterogeneous memory systems, which is relevant to the broader trend of building personal AI infrastructure. PAO uses a PostgreSQL entity-observation database and a Cloudflare Worker semantic search index as memory backends, fusing results with backend-specific retrieval strategies. It manages the full agent lifecycle including trust pre-seeding, readiness polling with error detection, and adaptive terminal text injection, and the paper documents three generations of context delivery mechanisms and their failure modes.

**Background**: LLM coding agents like Claude Code start each session with an empty context window, discarding accumulated knowledge from prior work. Persistent memory systems aim to solve this by storing and retrieving relevant information across sessions. PAO builds on this idea by pre-loading memories at spawn time, using a combination of structured and semantic search backends, and injecting the context via filesystem configuration auto-read behavior.

## [Interpretable Multimodal Classification with Linear Discriminant Tree Ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20384) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a tree-based ensemble framework for multimodal classification that uses Linear Discriminant Tree (LDT), Linear Discriminant Forest (LDF), and Linear Discriminant AdaBoost (LDAB) classifiers. These models achieve F1-mod gains of 4.3% over the Multimodal Transformer and accuracy gains of 3.0% over the interpretable baseline IMR, while also improving human-annotator agreement on feature importance. This work addresses a critical trade-off between accuracy and interpretability in multimodal learning, which is essential for trust-sensitive applications like clinical monitoring and educational assessment. By providing human-understandable explanations without sacrificing performance, it could enable wider adoption of AI in domains where transparency is mandatory. The framework encodes each modality into tokens, extracts and clusters concepts to reduce dimensionality, and uses a modified feature importance metric that reduces the influence of the negative class in binary tasks. The proposed feature importance achieves higher human-annotator agreement scores than default importance (62.2% vs. 43.2% on IEMOCAP; 46.7% vs. 32.1% on CMU-MOSI).

This position paper critiques the maximalist values of current generative AI and proposes five design principles—restraint, sufficiency, selectivity over retention, material visibility, and friction as affordance—under the umbrella of 'Slow AI' to center environmental sustainability. This matters because it offers a structured framework to reshape generative AI design toward sustainability, potentially influencing developers, policymakers, and users to prioritize environmental values over frictionless defaults. It addresses a timely gap in AI ethics and sustainability discourse. Each principle operates at two levels: a design implementation and an interpretive layer that prompts reflective engagement. ## [STCO: Conditional Neural Operators for Time-Dependent PDEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20477) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Spatiotemporal Conditional Operator (STCO), a framework for prescribed-condition operator learning in time-dependent PDEs, featuring a novel condition interface combining Flow-Aware Graph Leaf (FAGL) and Dual-Site Feature-wise Linear Modulation (DSFiLM). STCO addresses a gap in neural operators by enabling prescribed-condition learning, which is crucial for control and optimization tasks. It demonstrates significant error reductions across multiple backbone architectures, potentially advancing surrogate modeling for physical systems. The method uses non-learned FAGL to construct adaptive partitions from vorticity, and DSFiLM to inject motion, inflow, and force conditions. Evaluated on twelve matched backbones, it achieves mean paired reductions of 31.1% in relative-L2 field error and 24.7% in normalized pressure-derived load error.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Neural operators are machine learning models that learn mappings between function spaces, often used as surrogates for PDE solvers. Traditional models condition predictions only on observed states and static descriptors, but for control tasks, conditions like body motion or forcing are prescribed at query time. STCO introduces a common interface to supply these prescribed conditions to various backbone architectures.

## [Survey Frames Terminal Agents with Seven-Dimensional Competence Profile](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20485) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This survey introduces a novel organizing framework for terminal agents, defining them as systems whose action-observation loop is mediated by terminal command execution, and proposes a seven-dimensional competence profile for evaluation. It synthesizes system architecture, competence acquisition, and evaluation through the lens of terminal-mediated execution. This framework provides a unified basis for studying terminal agents across software engineering and emerging application domains, addressing the fragmentation of existing research. It highlights the importance of process-level evidence and explicit reporting of system conditions, which could improve reproducibility and comparability in AI agent research. The survey establishes workload-level boundaries and shows that realized behavior is jointly shaped by the model, interface, harness, runtime, and environment. Bounded fixed-condition diagnostics reveal that benchmark families expose different process signals, and matched system comparisons show benchmark-dependent performance and limits of component attribution.
