## [FL-MAESTRO: Multi-Agent LLM Orchestration for Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20518) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FL-MAESTRO introduces a multi-agent LLM orchestrator that makes joint runtime decisions in federated learning, using three specialist LLM agents to decide communication topology, per-client resource allocation, and aggregation rules. It cuts wasted round energy from over a third to near zero on a non-IID CIFAR-10 benchmark while matching the accuracy of the strongest energy-aware baseline. This work addresses a critical gap in federated learning by jointly optimizing runtime decisions that were previously handled separately or at setup time. It demonstrates the potential of LLM agents to improve efficiency in resource-constrained edge environments, which is increasingly important as FL scales to heterogeneous devices. The orchestrator consumes the server's predicted-failure list to withhold clients whose updates would never be aggregated, removing the dominant source of wasted round energy. Client state is read as natural-text profiles, allowing extension to heterogeneous device classes without per-class energy models. Code is available at https://github.com/denoslab/FL-MAESTRO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: In federated learning, communication topology is a runtime variable because links and edge devices drop in and out during training. Each round, the server must decide communication topology, per-client resource allocation, and aggregation rules, which are coupled decisions. Recent agentic systems have begun bringing LLMs into FL, but existing work either operates at setup time or handles a single runtime dimension. FL-MAESTRO addresses this by using a multi-agent orchestrator to make joint runtime decisions.

## [DASO: Difficulty-Aware Semantic-ID Optimization for Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Difficulty-Aware Semantic-ID Optimization (DASO), a tree-aware post-training method that addresses reward degeneration in GRPO by reallocating online rollouts based on prefix-match depth. DASO improves over MiniOneRec-style GRPO on 11 of 12 metrics and achieves the best result on 9 of 12 metrics on public benchmarks. This work addresses a critical failure mode in applying GRPO to tree-structured semantic-ID generation, where vanilla GRPO often fails to provide meaningful reward signals. By improving post-training efficiency and effectiveness, DASO can enhance the performance of generative recommendation systems, benefiting both researchers and practitioners in the field. DASO profiles each rollout group by prefix-match depth to identify bottleneck SID levels, then reallocates a bounded portion of the group to prefix-guided completions while retaining raw rollouts for contrast. It uses a SID-prefix reward for graded credit and an auxiliary SFT anchor to mitigate regression on already-solved examples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic-ID-based generative recommendation casts retrieval and ranking as autoregressive generation over hierarchical item identifiers, often trained with supervised fine-tuning (SFT) followed by GRPO. GRPO is a policy-gradient RL method that avoids a learned value critic, but it can suffer from reward degeneration when on-policy groups lack exact target completions. DASO is designed to overcome this by dynamically allocating rollouts based on difficulty.

## [Weighted Memory Tree Boosts LLM Agent Long-Horizon Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Weighted Memory Tree (WMT), a hierarchical memory system that assigns dynamic retention scores to memories, enabling LLM agents to preserve useful information and suppress irrelevant content. On GAIA-Text, WMT improves accuracy by 9.97 percentage points on average over linear memory while reducing prompt-token usage by 32.8%. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in long-horizon LLM agents: managing growing execution histories without degrading reasoning quality. By focusing on which memories remain active rather than storing more, WMT offers a practical approach that could improve the efficiency and reliability of AI agents across various applications. WMT organizes execution into tasks, subtasks, and actions, with event-based updates and selection-based decay revising retention scores. Memory-poisoning experiments show that WMT limits the persistence and propagation of unreliable information, and ablations confirm the importance of dynamic retention scoring.

**Background**: LLM agents often rely on external memory to handle long-horizon tasks, but existing memory systems struggle to decide which information to keep active. Hierarchical memory systems, such as HORMA, organize experiences into structured hierarchies to improve performance. WMT extends this idea by introducing dynamic retention scores that adapt based on usage and relevance.

The paper introduces the Knowledge-Driven Analytics Framework (KDAF) with Context-Aware Relevance Propagation (CARP) for auditable retrieval-augmented generation in enterprise finance. Evaluated on FinanceBench, KDAF achieves the highest citation traceability F1 (0.515), outperforming baselines while maintaining comparable accuracy. This work addresses the critical trust gap in LLM adoption for regulated financial workflows by prioritizing auditability alongside accuracy. It provides a practical framework that could enable broader use of LLMs in finance and other regulated industries where traceability is mandatory. On FinanceBench (145 questions), KDAF's accuracy is statistically indistinguishable from BM25 (difference -0.007, 95% CI [-0.021, 0.000]), but its citation traceability F1 is significantly higher (0.515 vs BM25's 0.463, CI [0.024, 0.083]). Graph-structured retrieval ensures no evidence from outside the question subject entity (0 of 426 items), and every selected item has a complete provenance chain.

## [Why2Speak: Faithful Reasoning for Abstaining Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20670) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Why2Speak, a framework to study the tradeoff between action quality and reasoning transparency in agentic systems, using multi-party conversation as a testbed. It compares direct policies, reasoning policies, supervised fine-tuning, and reinforcement learning on Qwen3-8B, finding that direct policies outperform reasoning policies but lack auditability. This research addresses a critical challenge in AI safety and interpretability: ensuring that reasoning traces faithfully reflect the computation behind an agent's actions, especially for abstention decisions. The findings highlight a capability-auditability tradeoff that could impact the design of oversight mechanisms for autonomous systems. The study uses Qwen3-8B with and without chain-of-thought reasoning, and finds that supervised fine-tuning either suppresses reasoning or preserves it without improving decision quality, while reinforcement learning fails to improve reasoning policies. It also identifies that group relative objectives provide no learning signal on confidently wrong prompts when all sampled rollouts select the same action, and that standard faithfulness methods can overstate evidence of reasoning fidelity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic systems are autonomous AI systems that can reason, plan, and execute multi-step tasks with minimal human intervention. Chain-of-thought reasoning is a prompting technique that elicits intermediate reasoning steps from language models, which can be used for interpretability. In multi-party conversations, an agent must decide when to speak or remain silent, which involves intervention timing and abstention decisions.

## [DirEAG: Dirichlet Evidence Aggregation for Calibrating Verbalized Confidence in Math Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20717) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DirEAG is a new method that converts each elicited answer-confidence observation from LLMs into calibrated soft evidence over candidate answers and a null state, using Dirichlet evidence aggregation. It is evaluated on GSM8K, SVAMP, and GSM-Hard with Qwen, Mistral, and Gemma models, showing improved calibration over direct averaging and heuristic aggregation. This work addresses a critical challenge in uncertainty estimation for LLMs in mathematical reasoning, where verbalized confidence is often miscalibrated. By providing a principled aggregation method, it could improve the reliability of LLM outputs in high-stakes applications that require trustworthy confidence estimates. DirEAG models a null state to represent cases where none of the candidate answers is correct, and ablations show that evidence aggregation and final binary calibration address distinct parts of the calibration problem. The method is designed for black-box LLMs and does not require access to internal logits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often provide verbalized confidence scores, but these are poorly calibrated, especially in mathematical reasoning tasks. Existing black-box uncertainty methods rely on answer agreement or entropy, which do not capture the numerical meaning of self-reported confidence. Dirichlet distribution is a probability distribution over probability vectors, useful for modeling uncertainty in evidence aggregation.

## [ForeTime-VLA Distills Future-Aware Representations for Conveyor-Belt Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20735) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ForeTime-VLA is a new dense pi0.5 policy that distills future-aware, action-equivalent representations from a frozen Fast-WAM teacher into a causal VLA policy, enabling efficient conveyor-belt manipulation without deploying the world model at inference. It achieves 81.1% stationary and 58.9% slow-moving grasp success, outperforming baselines by 12.2 and 22.2 percentage points respectively. This work addresses a practical challenge in dynamic manipulation—anticipating contact events with moving objects—without the computational cost of running a video-scale teacher at deployment. It demonstrates a viable path to improving VLA policies for real-world industrial tasks like conveyor-belt picking, which could impact robotics and automation industries. The method compresses current and future video latents into a whitened 64-D target, predicted by an eight-frame history encoder along with manipulation phase and normalized time-to-transition. Four future tokens and one phase token condition the VLM prefix, while the predicted future and transition horizon condition the action expert; training adds cosine, relational geometry, phase, time-to-transition, and action-equivalence objectives to the original flow-matching action target.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) policies typically generate actions from current observations, but manipulating moving objects requires anticipating future contact events. World action models (WAMs) learn predictive dynamics by jointly modeling future states and actions, but they are computationally expensive to run at deployment. Flow matching is a generative technique used in VLA action heads to transform noise into action trajectories via ODE-based velocity fields. ForeTime-VLA distills future-aware knowledge from a WAM into a causal policy, balancing performance and efficiency.

This paper introduces a paired weight-delta path audit to analyze how medical specialization updates are distributed across model components, applied to two generalist-to-medical-specialist checkpoint pairs: Gemma-3-4B-IT to MedGemma-4B-IT and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct to HuatuoGPT-o1-7B. The audit finds that decoder-side updates strongly reconstruct benchmark movement (endpoint-normalized retention of 0.974 and 1.183) but are not cleanly localized. The audit separates update-level reconstruction from component-level explanation, finding that MLP is the strongest broad component family in both pairs, but mixed off-domain movements, 10-seed matched controls, and endpoint-anchored rollbacks prevent a unique coarse-family explanation. The claims concern text-only multiple-choice benchmark movement, not clinical validation or circuit-level mechanism.

## [Asymmetric Structural Conditioning Improves Multi-Agent Document Authoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20786) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a deployed tender-response system using an open-weights model under sovereignty constraints, and reports that on a blind comparison, an LLM judge rated its answers at least as good as human-written bids on 40 of 55 sections. It also reveals that 68% of identified gaps were due to missing information, not writing quality, and that converting instruction material from prose to nested XML dropped answer quality from 74% to 48%. This work highlights a crucial distinction between information availability and writing quality in LLM evaluation, which can lead to more accurate assessments of multi-agent systems. The asymmetric conditioning insight—using structure for reading but prose for writing—offers practical guidance for designing document authoring pipelines, potentially improving efficiency and quality in formal document generation. The system runs an open-weights model under sovereignty constraints and was evaluated against human-written bids. The study found that naming a forbidden construction concentrates rather than removes defects, with 96% of surviving defects falling into the two explicitly named forms, and that coupling stochastic annotation to a deterministic windowing function reduced extracted requirement count from 68 to 51 on a byte-identical file.

<a id="item-153"></a>
## [Recall-Anchored Distillation Prevents Factual Access Failures in LLM SFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20794) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Recall-Anchored Distillation (RAD), a base-anchored self-distillation objective that preserves out-of-distribution generation behavior by aligning the adapted model with the original base model's soft continuation distribution on unlabeled OOD text. RAD requires no gold OOD answers, external judges, or labeled factual data. This work identifies a novel phenomenon, factual access failure, where models can recognize correct answers under constrained evaluation but fail to generate them in closed-book settings after domain SFT. RAD offers a practical solution that recovers lost OOD recall while preserving target-domain adaptation, which is significant for improving the reliability of fine-tuned LLMs in real-world applications. Across three backbones fine-tuned on MedMCQA, RAD recovers a consistent portion of the lost OOD recall while preserving target-domain adaptation. Compared with replay on the same OOD text, RAD shows that the key preservation signal is the base model's soft distribution rather than additional text exposure alone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) can degrade factual behavior outside the target domain, often described as catastrophic forgetting. However, this paper distinguishes a more specific phenomenon: factual access failure, where the underlying facts are not erased but become inaccessible during open-ended generation. Distillation is a technique where a smaller or adapted model learns from a larger or original model's outputs, and self-distillation uses the model itself as the teacher. RAD leverages the base model's soft distribution to preserve OOD generation behavior without needing labeled data.

## [CRATE: Two-Stage VLM-as-Judge for Mobile Agent Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20797) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CRATE is a novel two-stage VLM-as-judge framework that evaluates mobile agents via step-level consequence reasoning and trajectory-level aggregation, addressing context overload and operational safety. It achieves an F1-score of 0.833 on AndroidWorld, outperforming SPA-Bench by 20%, and extends to CRATE-S for safety assessment with an F1-score of 0.697 on MobileRisk. This framework improves automated evaluation of mobile agents by reducing context overload and incorporating safety assessment, which are critical for real-world deployment. It is compatible with both open- and closed-source models, making it broadly applicable and potentially setting a new standard for agent evaluation. CRATE uses Qwen2.5-VL-72B-Instruct as the judge model and performs step-level consequence reasoning to extract task-relevant visual clues and infer action-conditioned state changes. The step-level evidence is aggregated at the trajectory level for task completion evaluation, and CRATE-S extends this to operational safety assessment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Mobile agents are AI systems that interact with mobile interfaces to complete tasks. Traditionally, their performance was evaluated using rule-based methods, but these are not scalable. Model-based evaluation, such as VLM-as-judge, uses a multimodal model to assess outputs, but existing methods process entire trajectories at once, causing context overload and often ignoring safety. CRATE addresses these issues by breaking down evaluation into steps and aggregating evidence.

## [Dynamic Context Scheduling Boosts Contextual RL Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20799) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces dynamic context scheduling as a training method for contextual reinforcement learning, where context evolves within each training episode according to a predetermined schedule. The proposed framework, DYNAMICCARLENV, wraps contextual environments with pluggable schedule families and shows improved out-of-distribution and in-distribution performance on CartPole, BipedalWalker, and VehicleRacing. This work addresses the challenge of out-of-distribution generalization in reinforcement learning, which is critical for deploying RL agents in real-world scenarios where environments vary dynamically. By treating context variation as a controlled shaping mechanism, it offers a novel and potentially widely applicable training paradigm that could improve robustness and adaptability of RL agents. The framework supports pluggable schedule families such as sinusoidal offsets and cosine annealing, and the paper shows that dynamic schedules match or outperform static context baselines in OOD regimes. For more complex environments like BipedalWalker and VehicleRacing, dynamic schedules also achieve higher in-distribution evaluation performance, and automatic search for multi-stage curricula can discover schedules comparable to extensive grid search.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Contextual reinforcement learning (cRL) models tasks with varying context parameters, enabling flexible task specification. Out-of-distribution generalization in RL refers to the ability of an agent to perform well in environments that differ from those seen during training, which is a known challenge. This paper proposes a training strategy that dynamically schedules context changes within episodes to expose the policy to a richer parameter space.

## [SPARC: Single-Pass Bayesian-Conformal Uncertainty for Motion Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20802) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SPARC introduces a Bayesian-conformal uncertainty layer that provides calibrated, structured uncertainty estimates for motion forecasting in a single forward pass, eliminating the need for repeated stochastic inference. It combines a conjugate Bayesian last layer with split conformal calibration to produce 95% prediction tubes with finite-sample validity. This work addresses a critical need for reliable uncertainty quantification in safety-critical applications like autonomous driving, where calibrated and efficient uncertainty estimates are essential for risk assessment. By achieving state-of-the-art performance on NLL and combined metrics without sacrificing point accuracy, SPARC offers a practical solution that could be widely adopted in the motion forecasting community. SPARC uses a deterministic MLP backbone to predict future means and a conjugate Bayesian last layer to compute an analytic epistemic scale κ_t(x), which inflates a graph-temporal Gaussian covariance without altering its correlation structure. The method ranks first on NLL and combined MPJPE+NLL across nine dataset-protocol blocks, and the κ scale can serve as a lightweight risk monitor by ranking high-error windows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Motion forecasting is crucial for autonomous driving and robotics, requiring accurate predictions of future trajectories. Uncertainty quantification in deep learning typically falls into two categories: aleatoric (data-inherent) and epistemic (model-knowledge) uncertainty. Bayesian and ensemble methods often require multiple stochastic inferences, while conformal prediction provides distribution-free calibration but lacks epistemic signals and covariance structure preservation. SPARC combines these approaches to achieve both efficiency and structured uncertainty.

## [MGAL: Multilingual Granularity-Aware Long-Context Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20853) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MGAL is introduced as the first multilingual, granularity- and position-aware long-context benchmark, built from UN reports spanning 8K to 128K tokens across six official UN languages. It evaluates LLMs at four granularity levels (word, sentence, paragraph, document) and by position (begin, middle, end), enabling fine-grained diagnosis. This benchmark fills a gap in long-context evaluation, which has been mostly document-level and high-resource language-focused. It enables systematic assessment of LLMs' multilingual comprehension across granularities, revealing strengths and weaknesses that could guide model development and evaluation practices. Experiments show LLMs excel at word-level tasks but struggle with coarser granularities, and closed-source models maintain an advantage in lower-resource languages. Two new challenges are identified: local semantic crowding causing reliance on surface cues, and a gap between fluency and consistency in generated outputs.

**Background**: Long-context LLM evaluation has advanced, but existing benchmarks often focus on document-level tasks and high-resource languages, missing fine-grained challenges. MGAL addresses this by using UN reports, which are naturally multilingual and available in six official languages, and by stratifying entries by granularity and position. This design allows for a more nuanced understanding of model capabilities across different linguistic and contextual levels.

## [Coverage-Driven Verification for Safety-by-Design in AI Collision Avoidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20864) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a structured method for assessing the representativeness of Operational Design Domains (ODD) in AI-based collision avoidance systems, using Kullback-Leibler divergence and Cramér's V instead of the chi-squared test for large datasets. The method is demonstrated with experimental data from Horizontal and Vertical Collision Avoidance System simulations. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety assurance for aviation, providing a practical engineering process aligned with EASA's learning assurance objectives. It enables developers to systematically verify that AI/ML systems operate safely within their defined ODD, which is essential for certification and deployment in safety-critical applications. The chi-squared goodness-of-fit test was found unsuitable for large datasets, leading to the adoption of Kullback-Leibler divergence and Cramér's V as quantitative measures. The method guides developers from ODD definition and parameter distribution modeling to quantitative assessment and interpretation of coverage results, contributing to a Safety-by-Design AI engineering process.

**Background**: Operational Design Domain (ODD) refers to the specific conditions under which an automated system is designed to operate safely, such as road types, weather, and speed. The European Union Aviation Safety Agency (EASA) emphasizes the need to demonstrate the representativeness and completeness of ODD and associated data distributions for AI/ML-based systems in aviation. Coverage-driven verification is a methodology that uses coverage metrics to guide testing and ensure that all relevant scenarios are exercised.

## [UpgradeBench: A New Benchmark for Upgrading Fine-Tuned LLM Specialists](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20918) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UpgradeBench is a novel decision-centric benchmark that evaluates strategies for upgrading fine-tuned LLM specialists across successive model releases, covering four Qwen releases, one continuation checkpoint, six tasks, and two model sizes, plus OLMo checkpoints. It reveals that upgrade gains vary by task and release, with some retrained baselines improving while others stay within training noise. This benchmark addresses a practical and timely problem in LLM deployment: how to efficiently upgrade task-specific adapters when base models are updated. It provides systematic empirical insights that can help organizations reduce compute and labeling costs while avoiding performance regressions, which is valuable for practitioners and researchers in the LLM ecosystem. The benchmark disentangles three core questions: whether a new checkpoint improves fixed-recipe retrained specialist performance, whether specialization assets transfer across versions, and what recovery resources are usable. Key findings include that direct adapter copying depends on continued-pretraining distance (retention drops from 0.88-0.99 at 46B tokens to zero at 2.9T tokens on OLMo), and a lightweight CKA probe predicts portability with Spearman 0.74 across eight model pairs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Fine-tuned LLM specialists are task-specific adapters built on top of open-weight language models, which are models whose parameters are publicly available for download and fine-tuning. When a new base model is released, organizations must decide whether to retain existing specialists, port adapters, refresh from preserved behavior, or retrain. Benchmarks in machine learning are standardized tests used to evaluate and compare the performance of models or strategies, and UpgradeBench is designed specifically for the upgrade decision process.

## [Counterfactual Receipts Ensure AI Evaluators Reason, Not Just Label](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20938) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a formal framework for evaluator reasoning accountability, defining judgment receipts as minimal source replacement sets and presenting ReasonBench, a benchmark with 19,520 cases and 7,200 controls. Experiments show that standard accuracy masks severe robustness flaws, with meaning-preserving permutations reducing valid receipt recovery to 54.8%. This work addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation reliability, especially for agentic systems that rely on evaluators for gating actions, routing reviews, or training feedback. By decoupling prediction from certification, it provides a path toward trustworthy evaluator auditing, which is essential as AI systems become more autonomous. The framework defines three core sources of judgment: grounds, norms, and authority, which form an eight-cell counterfactual judgment cube. The paper also derives certification cost bounds for black-box evaluators and shows that structured counterfactual supervision fails to guarantee robust reasoning, as permutation retraining boosts consistency to 96.6% but worsens cube prediction deficits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: AI evaluators are models that assess the outputs of other AI systems, often providing labels such as 'good' or 'bad'. Traditional evaluation only checks whether the final label is correct, ignoring whether the reasoning behind the label is valid. This paper introduces counterfactual receipts, which are minimal changes to the input sources that would alter the judgment, to make reasoning transparent and auditable.

## [TLive-Omni: Omni-Modal Model for E-Commerce Live Streaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20958) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TLive-Omni is a new omni-modal understanding model designed for e-commerce live streaming, which integrates image, video, audio, and text inputs into a unified representation space. It introduces Per-vGrid for temporal alignment and Faithful-RFT, a reinforcement fine-tuning stage that improves answer faithfulness and real-time performance. This work addresses the practical challenge of understanding noisy, temporally extended live-streaming content, which is relevant to the growing e-commerce and AI industries. By improving omni-modal understanding and real-time performance, it could enhance automated customer service, product recommendation, and content moderation in live commerce. The model uses a three-stage supervised training recipe and a synchronized length-grouped sampler to reduce padding while maintaining comparable workloads. Faithful-RFT scores final responses directly with task-verifiable feedback rather than optimizing for reasoning-style exploration, and a lightweight dynamic sampling strategy regenerates rollout groups with near-zero reward variance for GRPO.

## [Deep Learning Models Also Recall Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20970) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces 'feature recall' as a general operation in deep learning models, contrasting it with the established paradigm of feature combination. It defines feature recall, shows its applicability across architectures, and discusses how it might be mechanistically identified. This conceptual framework could influence mechanistic interpretability research by offering a new perspective on how deep learning models operate. It bridges philosophy and empirical research, potentially guiding future investigations into model internals. The core observation is that a linear projection can be read as retrieving stored information scaled by input activations. The paper argues that factual recall in large language models points to a broader operation applicable across architectures.

## [MOSAIC Benchmark Reveals VLMs Fail to Translate Theory of Mind into Action](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20975) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MOSAIC, a new benchmark that evaluates whether vision-language models (VLMs) can translate theory of mind (ToM) into coordinated social actions. Testing 13 models (including 11 VLMs) across 200 trials each, the authors find that current VLMs fail to produce behaviors consistent with ToM constraints, and that explicit ToM constraints do not reliably alter their behavior. This work addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation by measuring the link between social reasoning and social action, which is essential for developing embodied agents that can interact naturally. The finding that VLMs fail under ToM constraints highlights a significant limitation in current multimodal models, with implications for robotics, human-AI interaction, and social AI research. MOSAIC involves two embodied agents interacting in cooperative and competitive scenarios, requiring integration of verbal statements, spatial trajectories, gaze direction, and facial expressions under systematically varied ToM constraints. Signal-level analysis reveals two sequential bottlenecks: most models cannot produce directionally coherent nonverbal signals, and even when signals are present, VLM agents fail to interpret others' behaviors and react to them. Notably, PCM-LLM, which includes an explicit ToM module, succeeds across all conditions, suggesting that explicit belief-action coupling is sufficient for this class of tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Theory of mind (ToM) is the ability to attribute mental states—such as beliefs, intentions, and desires—to oneself and others, and it is crucial for social interaction. Vision-language models (VLMs) are multimodal AI systems that process both images and text, extending large language models with visual understanding. Existing ToM benchmarks typically evaluate reasoning in isolation or focus on false-belief tasks, without measuring whether models can use ToM to guide coordinated behavior in embodied settings. MOSAIC aims to fill this gap by testing both inference and action in a controlled environment.

## [COTA: Tiny Advisors for Runtime Intervention in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21027) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces COTA (Comparison-Only Tiny Advisor), a framework that uses a tiny comparator to judge whether sampled alternatives lead to better continuations than the actor's proposal, enabling constructive runtime intervention without retraining the actor. COTA improves all nine evaluation settings across WebShop, ALFWorld, and tau^3-Retail with three actors, outperforming baselines. This work addresses a practical problem in LLM agent reliability by showing that effective runtime intervention can be achieved with a much weaker auxiliary model, reducing cost and capacity demands. It offers a fresh approach that could make LLM agents more robust in real-world applications without the need for expensive expert solvers or large critics. The comparator is trained using pairwise supervision constructed from same-prefix counterfactual branches, and preferred alternatives are returned as non-binding advice, leaving the original actor to replan. The results demonstrate that constructive runtime intervention remains effective even when the auxiliary model has substantially weaker task-solving capability than the actor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly used for tasks requiring reasoning, tool use, and sequential decision-making. As they operate over longer horizons, runtime intervention can improve reliability without retraining the underlying model. Existing methods often rely on an expert solver or a task-capable critic, which are costly or capacity-heavy. COTA instead uses a tiny comparator that only compares alternatives, making it a lightweight and cost-effective solution.

## [Socialized Division and Collaboration: A New Paradigm for Class-Incremental Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21044) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Socialized Division and Collaboration (SDC), a novel framework for class-incremental learning that decomposes session learning across specialized models to address optimization conflicts, using an energy-based session-model compatibility criterion grounded in Helmholtz free energy. SDC offers an alternative to monolithic continual learning formulations, potentially reducing catastrophic forgetting and improving performance in dynamic environments. This could impact real-world applications where models must continuously learn new classes without forgetting old ones. The framework integrates session assignment, model evolution, and collaborative inference into a unified pipeline. It is motivated by social solidarity theory and uses an energy-based criterion to guide adaptive session allocation under conflicting objectives.

Energy-based models provide a framework for probabilistic modeling using energy functions, which can be used to measure compatibility between sessions and models.

## [Physics Prior Cost Bounded by Ablation Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21059) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that the accuracy cost of enforcing a physics prior is bounded by the ablation gap, yielding the inequality 0 ≤ P ≤ D for any risk functional. It also introduces a practical sign test: a constrained model must never be beaten by its own ablation. This result reframes the cost of physics priors as a property of the validation protocol and free features, not the prior itself, which could change how models are validated in scientific machine learning. The sign test offers a simple, assumption-free check for model validity, with broad applicability. The bound holds without convexity, smoothness, or realizability assumptions, and is empirically instantiated on a wildfire-severity task (N=26,681, K=3) with monotone constraints. The paper also shows that cost and compliance are independent, and proposes a two-fit screen to reject unidentifiable experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: In machine learning, a hypothesis class is a set of functions a model can choose from, and excess risk measures the gap between a model's error and the best possible error. Shape-constrained or physics-informed learning enforces priors like monotonicity, which can reduce accuracy; this paper shows that this cost is bounded by the ablation gap, the performance drop when removing the constrained features.

## [OAttention: New Attention Mechanism with Token-Level Active-Presence Coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21174) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OAttention, a novel attention mechanism that assigns each token an active-presence coefficient p_i = ||h_i||^2 / (τ + ||h_i||^2), which gates information emission and determines token participation in shared computations. This enables exact null-token handling, self-attention insertion, and empty-support properties, extending traditional attention masks. OAttention could improve interpretability and control in transformer models by providing a representation-carried token state that is non-participating at the attention boundary, potentially benefiting tasks involving missing values or dynamic token participation. However, the lack of empirical validation and community discussion limits its immediate impact. The paper includes contract tests and a GPU evaluation, showing that calibrated hidden-carrier OAttention and Full-O variants change mean RMSE by +0.088% and +0.177% respectively in a zero-fine-tuning retrofit of a cloned pretrained TabPFN v3 regressor over 18 matched dataset-seed cases. A two-block ablation demonstrates that OAttention alone does not preserve a NULL state through ordinary host components, whereas the OTransformer path does.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Attention mechanisms in transformers use relation-level masks to control which query-source pairs interact, but they do not provide a token-level state that can be non-participating. The proposed active-presence coefficient introduces a smooth radial presence that gates information flow, potentially allowing for exact handling of zero-vector tokens and compositional closure properties.

SENTRY replaces subjective questionnaire-based risk scoring with a deterministic pipeline combining XGBoost and hybrid retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). This addresses a critical pain point in large financial institutions where risk assessments are subjective and easily gamed. By providing deterministic, explainable risk scores, SENTRY can improve auditability and consistency, potentially reducing major incidents and setting a precedent for ML in regulated environments. The system integrates structured operational metadata, application dependency graphs, and historical incident records with hybrid semantic and lexical search over change requests. The retrieval step compresses risk signals into a single scalar feature before model inference, preserving determinism and enabling SHAP-based explainability.

This paper introduces personalized privacy for LLMs, a new benchmark P3Bench, and an inference-time attention head intervention method called Repair to enforce user-specific disclosure policies. ## [Unified Branch-and-Bound Search for Steiner TSP on Convex Set Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21319) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper formalizes the Steiner Traveling Salesman Problem on Graphs of Convex Sets (Steiner-TSP on GCS) and proposes a unified branch-and-bound search over rooted walk prefixes, with optimality guarantees under a uniform positive-cost assumption. Both best-first and depth-first traversal strategies find feasible solutions on all benchmark instances within 30 seconds, with mean certified optimality gaps of 28.1% and 29.7%, respectively. This work extends the classical Steiner TSP to continuous convex sets, enabling joint optimization of discrete visitation order and continuous trajectories, which is crucial for robotics and inspection tasks. The unified branch-and-bound framework with theoretical guarantees provides a robust baseline for future research in combinatorial optimization and motion planning. The proposed method uses additive lower-bound-graph costs for committed prefixes and a cut-separated connected-flow relaxation for residual costs. Under a uniform positive-cost assumption, best-first traversal terminates finitely without an initial incumbent, while depth-first requires a finite incumbent; a global lower bound certifies an epsilon-optimal solution for user-specified epsilon >= 1.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: The Steiner Traveling Salesman Problem (Steiner TSP) is a variant of the classic TSP where only a subset of vertices are required, and the goal is to find a shortest walk visiting all required vertices and returning to the start, allowing revisits. Branch-and-bound is a systematic search method that maintains the lowest-cost path to a target, often used for solving combinatorial optimization problems. This paper generalizes Steiner TSP to graphs of convex sets, where each vertex is a convex region, and the cost is the length of a continuous trajectory through these sets.

## [New Diagnostic Framework Reveals Lexical Interference and Label Bias in Mental Health NLP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20353) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces TSS (Triple-Stream Stress probe), a multi-channel diagnostic framework, and DoD (Degree of Divergence), a difference-in-differences statistic, to detect and quantify lexical interference and label bias in mental health NLP classifiers. Across four English datasets (N=12,906), TSS reveals that adding lexical features reduces Macro-F1 on human-labeled data (mean drop 0.072, p<10^-4) but not on auto-labeled data. This work addresses a critical issue in computational mental health: classifiers often degrade under distribution shift due to label-source-specific shortcut learning. By providing a diagnostic audit framework, it helps researchers identify and mitigate label bias before making generalization claims, potentially improving the reliability of mental health NLP models. The headline DoD estimate is DoD(BC-A) = 0.0374, 95% CI [0.0097, 0.0651], p=0.0032, with instance-level bootstrap inference. A platform-stratified Twitter-only analysis reproduces the pattern (DoD-Tw(BC-A) = +0.096, p<0.001; DoD-Tw(AC-A) = -0.089, p<0.001). Interventional masking (pos_only) retains ~95-99% of Channel C's performance after destroying content words on human datasets, indicating the style channel does not rely primarily on lexical surface form.

**Background**: Computational mental health (CMH) classifiers often use text data labeled by human annotators or distant-supervision pipelines, which may reward different linguistic signals, leading to distribution shift and label bias. The proposed TSS framework decomposes text into three channels: lexical character n-grams, a morpho-syntactic channel, and a psycholinguistic style channel, to isolate the influence of each. The DoD statistic, adapted from econometrics, provides a formal test for label-source differences, helping to audit whether models rely on unintended shortcuts.

The paper introduces Clarify-Then-Search, a benchmark with 518 curated instances built from Baidu search engine queries, to evaluate whether LLM-generated clarification questions improve deep search utility. It uses evidence-grounded nuggets and a weighted nugget-recall score (restore_score_100) for end-to-end evaluation. This benchmark addresses a critical problem in deep search: underspecified queries cause retrieval drift and incomplete answers. The benchmark includes a static golden reference built by running WebDancer once per intent query, with weighted, evidence-grounded nuggets and traceable source identifiers. Evaluation involves a Clarifier asking k questions (k=1,2,3), a closed-book User Answerer, and a Rewriter; results show clarification improves over baseline at k=1, with GPT-5.2 best at k=1 and ERNIE-4.5-Turbo-128K best at k=3. Diagnostics reveal a common failure: over-asking region-only questions that are often unanswerable from the intent.

## [VA-DPO: Continuous Valence-Arousal Control for Emotion Generation in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20374) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VA-DPO modifies Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to train language models to generate text with specified continuous valence-arousal (VA) emotional states. On Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, it reduces mean VA distance to the target by 33% over system prompting and 25% over few-shot prompting, while preserving general capabilities. This work enables more precise and nuanced emotional control in LLMs, moving beyond discrete emotion labels to continuous coordinates, which could improve applications like empathetic chatbots and creative writing. The method is simple and effective, showing that DPO can be adapted for fine-grained affective control without sacrificing performance on standard benchmarks. The method uses a frozen VA regressor to score sampled generations by Euclidean distance to the target, keeping only candidate pairs whose distance gap exceeds a margin tau, then optimizes a LoRA adapter with standard DPO loss. Gains generalize to Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.2-3B, with MMLU unchanged (Delta=+0.0) and HellaSwag and TruthfulQA preserved.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a reinforcement-learning-free method for aligning language models with human preferences by directly optimizing a policy against a reference model. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that trains small adapter modules instead of the full model. The Valence-Arousal model represents emotions on two continuous dimensions: valence (positive vs. negative) and arousal (calm vs. excited).

EditPPT introduces a multi-agent framework that reformulates slide editing as a constrained tool-selection problem, using the native PowerPoint COM interface and dual-modal validation. It also presents DeckEdit-Bench, a new benchmark with 28 human-authored decks, 582 slides, and 183 editing prompts across short, medium, and long deck tiers. This work addresses a practical problem in automating slide editing, which is crucial for productivity tools. By achieving high execution rates and robust performance on long decks, EditPPT could significantly improve the reliability of LLM-based presentation editing systems. EditPPT achieves a 99.5% execution rate, 88.7% slide-targeting F1, 82.5% instruction following, and 91.5% object preservation overall, while maintaining strong performance on long decks. The framework uses localized shape-level operations through the native PowerPoint COM interface to narrow the LLM action space and preserve the application-resolved structure.

## [Peer-Voted LLM-Agent Stress Tests Reveal Feed-Induced Lexical Convergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20438) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces PV-SST, a peer-voted social-platform testbed for LLM agents, and reports a preregistered matched-exposure experiment showing that exposure to peer-ranked posts increases lexical convergence in LLM agents. The robust effect is lexical convergence, not general opinion capture or coordination advantage. This work addresses the emerging area of population-level behavior in multi-agent LLM systems, which cannot be captured by single-agent benchmarks. It provides a rigorous, preregistered methodology and a novel testbed that could inform future studies on social dynamics and information diffusion in synthetic populations. The experiment comprised 448 trials and 112 complete model-by-topic-by-seed blocks across four topics, four seeds, four open-weight model families, and three larger variants. The core panel showed a paired mean difference of +0.0082 TF-IDF cosine units (95% CI [0.0043, 0.0121], p=0.000105) for lexical similarity, but the distributed-minus-single contrast was inconclusive, failing the prespecified consistency criterion.

**Background**: Large-language-model (LLM) agents are increasingly used to simulate social platforms, but population-level behaviors like lexical convergence are not captured by single-agent benchmarks. The study uses a preregistered design to reduce researcher bias and a matched-exposure setup to isolate the effect of feed content.

## [JuryProbe: Diagnosing Consensus Risk in Factuality Judge Panels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20607) ⭐️ 7.0/10

JuryProbe introduces an empirical diagnostic that detects consensus risk in reference-free factuality judge panels by measuring false-negative-only correlations and false-consensus lift, and routes flagged high-risk accepts to grounded verification with trusted references. This matters because it addresses a critical blind spot in LLM evaluation: correlated false negatives among reference-free judges can lead to false consensus and unsafe acceptances. The routing policy improves factuality verification efficiency and reliability, impacting AI safety and evaluation practices. On audited FEVER corruptions, reference-free panels showed high false-negative-only correlations (0.402 and 0.368) and lifts (3.13x and 18.13x), while unanimous false consensus dropped to zero under a trusted-reference best-case diagnostic. The routed policy is equivalent to grounding every reference-free majority accept in flagged settings, verified in 34/34 splits, and a fixed rule flags 8-10 of 10 splits while standing down on a negative control.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Reference-free factuality judge panels use multiple LLM judges to decide whether to accept or escalate claims without external references. However, agreement among judges may reflect shared blind spots rather than independent evidence, leading to false consensus. JuryProbe provides a diagnostic to quantify this risk and a routing policy to mitigate it by switching to grounded verification when risk is high.

## [AgenticRAG-FP: Benchmark for Causal Failure Attribution in Agentic RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20627) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces AgenticRAG-FP, an interventional benchmark for causal failure attribution in agentic retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). The benchmark injects a certified fault at a specified hop, re-executes the downstream trajectory, and evaluates diagnosers against the known intervention, revealing that post-hoc trace diagnosis fails to identify failures at later hops. This benchmark addresses a critical problem in multi-hop agentic systems, where retrieval errors can propagate and surface only at later hops. The empirical results show significant gaps in current diagnosis methods, highlighting practical challenges and likely influencing future research in RAG reliability and debugging. In the strict dense Claude Haiku 4.5 sweep on 80 three-hop MuSiQue questions, coverage-based diagnosis achieved 0.91 at hop 1 but 0.00 at hops 2 and 3 (n=43,36,21 failed trajectories). A smaller content-corruption study at depth 2 showed coverage-based diagnosis at 0.00 and a frozen-hop counterfactual probe at 0.67 in an exploratory pooled comparison.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic RAG systems interleave retrieval, reasoning, and answer generation across multiple hops, where a retrieval error at hop 1 can surface only as a wrong answer at hop 3, while later retrieval can also repair the trajectory. Causal failure attribution aims to identify which hop caused the final failure, which is crucial for debugging and improving such systems. The benchmark uses interventional testing by injecting known faults and re-executing the trajectory to evaluate diagnosers.

## [Provable Edge-of-Stability for Adam on a One-Dimensional Quadratic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20638) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper provides a rigorous theoretical analysis of the edge-of-stability (EoS) phenomenon in uncorrected Adam on a one-dimensional quadratic, proving a restoring tendency toward a frozen stability threshold and identifying cases where this mechanism fails. This work offers a concrete dynamical explanation for a widely observed but poorly understood behavior in Adam, a popular optimizer in deep learning. It bridges the gap between empirical observations and theoretical understanding, potentially guiding the design of more stable and efficient optimization algorithms. The paper characterizes Adam's dynamics across the parameter space, proving a restoring tendency toward the threshold 2(1+β1)/[η(1−β1)]. It also identifies breakdown cases, including strictly subcritical periodic orbits and specially tuned trajectories that converge to the optimum while remaining uniformly supercritical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: The edge-of-stability (EoS) phenomenon refers to the observation that during neural network training, the sharpness (largest eigenvalue of the Hessian) often hovers just above the stability threshold predicted by classical optimization theory. Adam is an adaptive learning rate optimizer that adjusts per-parameter learning rates based on gradient moments. This paper studies a simplified one-dimensional quadratic setting to isolate the optimizer-induced dynamics behind EoS, providing a clean theoretical framework.

The paper introduces PEMSB-3V, a new benchmark suite that preserves raw flow, speed, and occupancy data from PeMS detectors, and proposes RiskTraf, a model-agnostic risk-extrapolated residual plug-in that improves multivariate traffic flow prediction by learning a lightweight residual head from historical speed and occupancy. This work addresses a gap in traffic flow forecasting by leveraging raw multi-variate sensor data and mitigating regime-dependent shortcuts, potentially improving the robustness and accuracy of existing spatio-temporal models. It provides a new benchmark and a plug-in method that can be applied to diverse backbones, benefiting the traffic forecasting community. RiskTraf freezes the selected checkpoint of a trained spatio-temporal backbone and learns a zero-start residual head that constructs ordered traffic-risk environments and optimizes horizon-wise flow corrections with a risk extrapolation objective. Extensive experiments show consistent improvements over diverse backbones and outperform debiasing and distribution-shift adaptation methods.

Risk extrapolation (REx) is a robust optimization technique that penalizes variance of training risks to improve out-of-distribution generalization. ## [C-Score: A New Framework for Robustness Assessment in Semi-Supervised Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20667) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces C-Score, a compact framework that evaluates training behavior in three complementary spaces—prediction, feature representation, and optimization—to detect hidden collapse in pseudo-label-based semi-supervised learning under open-world unlabeled contamination. Experiments on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100 show that C-Score metrics reveal degradation that clean accuracy alone fails to detect. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating semi-supervised learning (SSL) robustness in real-world open environments, where unlabeled data often contain out-of-distribution (OOD) samples. By proposing a more reliable evaluation metric, it can help researchers and practitioners better assess and improve SSL methods, potentially leading to safer deployment in applications like autonomous driving and medical imaging. C-Score includes three specific metrics: PLE and CCI for unlabeled prediction behavior, Sem-Drift for deviation from labeled semantic anchors, and Grad-Align for compatibility between labeled and unlabeled optimization. Under SVHN contamination, CCI rises over 280% while best-accuracy remains within 3% of the uncontaminated baseline; near-OOD sources (CIFAR-100, STL-10) cause up to 14.9% accuracy collapse (FlexMatch, r=0.5).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Semi-supervised learning (SSL) leverages large amounts of unlabeled data alongside labeled data to improve model performance. Pseudo-label-based methods assign pseudo-labels to unlabeled samples and train on them, but they typically assume unlabeled data come from the same distribution as labeled data (closed-world). In open-world scenarios, unlabeled data may contain out-of-distribution (OOD) samples, which can receive high-confidence pseudo-labels and degrade learning, a phenomenon known as hidden collapse that accuracy metrics may miss.

## [LA-ReduNet: Lightweight Adaptive ReduNet via Hyperspherical Manifold Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20668) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces LA-ReduNet, a lightweight adaptive variant of ReduNet that employs hyperspherical manifold learning and adaptive step sizes to reduce the number of layers required for the MCR2 objective to reach a stable value. Under the considered experimental settings, LA-ReduNet requires only about 1/29 of the parameter storage of the unfolded ReduNet module while maintaining comparable classification accuracy. This work addresses a known limitation of ReduNet—the large number of layers needed for convergence—making white-box neural networks more practical for resource-constrained applications. It also demonstrates the effectiveness of hyperspherical manifold learning in improving the efficiency of representation learning, which could influence future designs of interpretable deep networks. LA-ReduNet refines the layer-wise update rule of ReduNet by incorporating hyperspherical manifold learning and adaptive step sizes, reducing the number of layers by an order of magnitude. The paper is a preprint (arXiv:2608.20668) and does not include community discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: ReduNet is a white-box neural network constructed from the principle of Maximal Coding Rate Reduction (MCR2), which aims to learn discriminative low-dimensional structures by maximizing the difference between global and per-class coding rates. Unlike traditional black-box networks trained via backpropagation, ReduNet derives each layer's parameters explicitly from the features of the previous layer, offering mathematical interpretability. However, the layer-wise construction often requires many layers for the MCR2 objective to stabilize, increasing parameter storage. Hyperspherical manifold learning is a technique that constrains representations to lie on a hypersphere, which has been shown to improve performance in tasks like face recognition.

## [Fuzzy-MoE: Interpretable Fuzzy Logic Routing for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20761) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Fuzzy-MoE, a novel Mixture-of-Experts model that uses fuzzy logic for interpretable expert routing in non-stationary multivariate time series forecasting. It employs a dual-view fuzzy router with learnable Gaussian membership functions to infer latent temporal states and activate experts via explicit IF-THEN rules. This work addresses the interpretability gap in MoE-based time series forecasting, where traditional black-box routing obscures which experts are used and why. By making routing transparent and rule-based, it could improve trust and diagnostics in critical applications like finance or energy forecasting, and inspire further research into interpretable dynamic models. Fuzzy-MoE consists of multiple parallel expert networks and a dual-view fuzzy router that jointly exploits local convolutional dynamics and global segmented statistics. The router uses learnable Gaussian membership functions to compute expert activation strengths, enabling fine-grained routing where different variables within the same sequence can activate different experts. Experiments on public benchmarks show significant accuracy improvements over mainstream methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a neural architecture that uses a gating network to route inputs to a subset of specialized expert networks, enabling sparse activation and scaling. However, traditional MoE routing is often a black box, lacking interpretability. Fuzzy logic provides a framework for reasoning with degrees of membership, allowing rules like IF-THEN to be applied to continuous inputs. This paper combines these ideas to create a transparent routing mechanism for time series forecasting, where non-stationarity and heterogeneity are common challenges.

## [SpeechLMs Suffer Previous-Belief Contamination; EmoUpdate Fixes It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20769) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies a novel failure mode, previous-belief contamination (PBC), in streaming emotion understanding, where historical predictions distort current audio perception. It introduces EmoUpdate, a training-free framework that improves state-balanced accuracy by up to 69.71 points across four SpeechLMs and two benchmarks. This work highlights a critical robustness issue in streaming speech models, which are increasingly used in real-time applications like virtual assistants and affective computing. By providing a training-free mitigation, it offers a practical solution that can be immediately applied to existing systems, potentially improving their reliability and trustworthiness. The counterfactual diagnostic on CREMA-D-Stream shows that changing only the injected previous emotion label while holding audio fixed drops accuracy from 72.50% to 30.42% and flips 65.69% of predictions. EmoUpdate comprises three components: a prior-blind acoustic firewall, an evidence-shrunk causal belief filter, and a closed-form decontamination operator for serving stacks where firewalling is unavailable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Streaming emotion understanding models process audio incrementally, using historical context to inform current predictions. However, feeding the model's previous prediction back as context can cause it to over-rely on prior beliefs, leading to biased perception. This paper addresses this issue by proposing a training-free framework that separates current-audio perception from historical state revision.

## [CAIRO: Context-Aware Item Profiling for LLM Recommenders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20801) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CAIRO, a user context-aware item profiling framework for LLM-based reranking, which structures raw metadata into objective features and subjective traits and uses a lightweight profiler to select the most relevant information for each user-item pair. Experiments show that CAIRO consistently improves LLM-based reranking performance. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM-based recommender systems: effectively leveraging vast, heterogeneous item metadata for personalized ranking. By making item profiling context-aware, CAIRO could enable more accurate and personalized recommendations, benefiting both users and platforms. CAIRO first structures raw metadata and reviews into objective features and subjective traits, then employs a lightweight profiler to select relevant information per user-item pair with limited serving-time overhead. The resulting profiles are concise and context-specific, providing relevant item-side evidence for the LLM's ranking decision.

## [FreqDiff: Frequency-Aware Diffusion for Temporal Knowledge Graph Extrapolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20804) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FreqDiff, a frequency-aware diffusion framework for temporal knowledge graph (TKG) extrapolation, which uses context-aware spectral calibration to better distinguish query-specific evidence from non-salient historical facts. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on four public TKG benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation in existing diffusion-based TKG extrapolation methods, where aggregated conditioning dilutes target-discriminative signals. By improving the accuracy of future fact prediction, FreqDiff could enhance applications such as event forecasting and decision support in dynamic environments. FreqDiff formulates future object prediction as query-slot denoising and uses a dual-stream denoiser that integrates temporal dependency modeling with context-aware spectral calibration. It also introduces a frequency-domain regularizer to align the denoised target with the gold object in spectral space.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal knowledge graphs (TKGs) represent facts that evolve over time, and TKG extrapolation aims to infer future facts from historical data. Diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise noisy data, and have been applied to TKG extrapolation to model uncertainty. However, existing methods often aggregate subject histories in a way that may not sufficiently distinguish query-specific evidence, leading to diluted signals. FreqDiff leverages frequency-domain analysis to adaptively recalibrate representations, improving the distinction between relevant and irrelevant historical information.

## [CERES: Coverage-Guided Re-Indexing Prevents Semantic Collapse in Generated Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20810) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CERES, a closed-loop multimodal indexing framework that builds a three-level semantic pyramid and uses coverage-guided re-indexing to prevent scale-specific concept loss in generated images. It achieves state-of-the-art results on four pansharpening benchmarks and improves concept-query retrieval Recall@5 by +14.0 points. This work addresses a critical yet overlooked problem in multimodal systems: generated images may look realistic but fail to be retrievable by the original queries due to semantic collapse. By ensuring generated content remains queryable, CERES enhances the reliability of generative perception and retrieval pipelines, impacting applications like image generation, search, and vision-language systems. CERES uses a co-occurrence-aware router to mine implicit concepts and performs scale-routed cross-attention into a lightweight U-Net generator with only 0.39M parameters. It employs a continuously differentiable soft-Jaccard coverage objective and verifies coverage via an independent DINOv2 linear probe trained on external labels, ensuring non-degeneracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic collapse in generative models refers to the failure to capture distinct semantic classes despite overall data diversity, often leading to generated images that lose scale-specific concepts. In multimodal systems, generated content is often routed back through the same vision-language index, so maintaining retrievability is crucial. CERES addresses this by re-indexing generated images with the same frozen VLM to verify coverage, a novel closed-loop approach.

## [STAR-OPD: On-Policy Reward Distillation for ABSA Quadruple Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20831) ⭐️ 7.0/10

STAR-OPD introduces an on-policy reward distillation method that trains student models on their own rollouts with cascade-aware, set-structured rewards, addressing structural errors in ABSA quadruple extraction. Experiments on E-ABSA20K and SemEval-2014 show it outperforms off-policy and general on-policy baselines, reducing target hallucination and improving performance on structurally hard cases. This work addresses a critical limitation in distilling large chain-of-thought models for ABSA quadruple extraction, a task with practical applications in sentiment analysis. By improving structural correctness and narrowing the student-teacher gap, it enables more efficient deployment of smaller models without sacrificing accuracy, which is valuable for real-world NLP systems. STAR-OPD builds on generic on-policy distillation and instantiates it for ABSA quadruple extraction with cascade-aware, set-structured rewards that target binding consistency, target grounding, and fine-grained aspect disambiguation. Using Qwen3-4B, it substantially narrows the student-teacher gap while improving inference efficiency, highlighting the importance of on-policy structural correction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Aspect-based sentiment analysis (ABSA) quadruple extraction requires jointly predicting target, aspect, opinion, and sentiment from reviews, often containing multiple fine-grained sentiment tuples. Large chain-of-thought (CoT) models perform well but are expensive to deploy, so knowledge distillation is used to create smaller models. However, conventional off-policy distillation trains only on teacher-generated trajectories, failing to supervise student-induced structural states that dominate inference, leading to errors like broken target-aspect bindings and hallucinated targets.

## [BC-Bench: New Benchmark for AI Agents in ERP Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20851) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BC-Bench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate agentic engineering systems on real-world ERP development tasks in Microsoft's AL language, comprising 101 manually curated tasks from two production repositories. It extends SWE-Bench methodology to include test generation and multimodal problem statements, and evaluates multiple frontier models across two agent harnesses. This benchmark addresses the underexplored area of agentic engineering in ERP domain-specific languages, providing a domain-specific evaluation that reveals performance gaps not captured by general-purpose benchmarks. It highlights the need for tailored evaluation and could guide future development of AI agents for enterprise software. The benchmark includes 101 tasks covering code generation, test generation, and multimodal problem statements, and uses multi-run metrics to account for nondeterminism. Results show that between-model differences in bug-fixing resolution rate are larger than differences between agent harnesses, and improvements on general benchmarks do not consistently transfer to AL.

**Background**: Agentic engineering systems are AI-driven tools that autonomously perform software development tasks, such as code generation and bug fixing. SWE-Bench is a well-known benchmark for software engineering tasks, and BC-Bench adapts its methodology to the AL ecosystem.

## [Source-Free MT Evaluation Is Not MT Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20925) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This position paper argues that machine translation (MT) evaluation should judge adequacy against the source sentence, not the reference translation, and that reference-based metrics are unfaithful and unfair. It calls for quality estimation (QE) to be reframed as a primary approach to source-grounded adequacy evaluation. This challenges the standard practice in MT evaluation, which relies heavily on reference-based metrics like BLEU. If adopted, it could shift how MT systems are developed and compared, making evaluation more faithful to the actual goal of preserving source meaning. The paper shows that existing hybrid metrics are highly reliant on the reference compared to the source. It argues that source-reference-hypothesis evaluation is only fair when the reference is treated as auxiliary evidence, not the primary standard.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Machine translation evaluation traditionally uses automatic metrics like BLEU, which compare the system output to one or more human reference translations. Quality estimation (QE) methods, which evaluate without references, often correlate less well with human judgments, so they are used mainly when references are unavailable. This paper argues that adequacy—whether the translation preserves the source meaning—should be judged directly against the source, not the reference.

The paper introduces Neural-Primitive, an end-to-end local planner that uses imitation learning on offline trajectory primitives to directly map sensory inputs to polynomial coefficients, achieving collision-free flight in real time without back-end solving. It reports ultra-fast computation (below 1ms on a desktop, average 3.68ms onboard) and low memory usage (less than 1.5MiB). This work addresses the computation-quality-memory trilemma in onboard trajectory generation, a key challenge for autonomous flight in cluttered environments. By enabling real-time, low-resource planning, it could advance applications like drone navigation and search-and-rescue, and its zero-shot sim-to-real transfer suggests practical deployability. The method uses a lightweight offline-primitive-based dataset collection framework to generate safe, high-quality trajectory primitives in non-convex environments. The neural network outputs polynomial coefficients that encode higher-order dynamics, and extensive simulations plus real-world experiments validate its performance.

## [Jacobian-Guided Noise Injection Boosts LLM Quantization Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20988) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Jacobian-Guided Noise Injection, a training strategy that injects zero-mean Gaussian noise into pre-attention logits with variance derived from the Jacobian Frobenius norm, to improve quantization robustness in LLMs. It achieves up to +37% relative Top-1 accuracy gains on ImageNet-1K for SigLIP and up to 40% relative perplexity improvement on WikiText for language models in low-bit settings. Quantization is critical for deploying LLMs efficiently, but self-attention is often sensitive to discretization errors. This work provides a principled, theoretically grounded method to enhance robustness, potentially enabling more aggressive model compression without significant performance loss, benefiting edge deployment and resource-constrained environments. The method identifies the softmax operator as a key bottleneck for quantization stability due to its sensitivity to outliers and state-dependent Jacobian. The noise variance is derived directly from the Jacobian Frobenius norm, offering a way to determine optimal noise based on local attention sensitivity, unlike heuristic approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Quantization reduces model size and speeds up inference by using lower-precision numbers, but it can degrade performance, especially in attention mechanisms. The Jacobian matrix describes how a function's output changes with respect to its input; for softmax, its norm indicates sensitivity to input perturbations. By suppressing this norm during training, the model becomes more robust to quantization noise.

## [CoST: Aligning Spatial and Temporal Semantics for Urban Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21041) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoST introduces a contrastive spatial-temporal framework that aligns spatial context with multi-temporal semantics to improve cross-region generalization and semantic interpretability in geospatial representation learning. It achieves an average relative gain of 8.7% over the strongest competing methods across eight city-indicator settings. This work addresses critical challenges in geospatial representation learning, such as cross-region generalization and semantic interpretability, which are essential for large-scale urban analysis and downstream applications. By aligning spatial and temporal semantics, CoST could enable more robust and transferable models, benefiting fields like urban planning, disaster response, and environmental monitoring. CoST explicitly models spatial correlations to capture transferable geographic structures and exploits multi-year urban change semantics to align learned representations with high-level geo-semantics. The code is available in a public repository, and the framework is evaluated on various downstream tasks and unseen scenarios.

## [Z^2-ACT: Verifiable Agentic Intent Control for Open 6G RAN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21049) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Z^2-ACT, an end-to-end architecture that integrates zero-knowledge auditable control and zero-trust verification for agentic intent control in open 6G RAN. It combines four primitives—agentic coordination, formal intent constraints, zero-trust prompt verification, and cryptographic accountability—across non-real-time and near-real-time RICs. This work addresses a critical gap in 6G RAN security and verifiability, offering a unified solution that ensures AI-assisted control loops remain safe and auditable under concurrent operator intents and untrusted inputs. It could influence future network standards and enhance trust in multi-vendor, AI-driven RAN deployments. The architecture encodes operator goals as typed Intent Contracts, admits LLM inputs only after adversarial intent checks, and releases skill sequences only when a self-management gate is satisfied. Every successful commit is recorded as a binding commitment with a zero-knowledge proof, and experiments on ColO-RAN data show improved actuation filtering and attack resilience at modest latency and signaling cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Open and disaggregated 6G RANs aim to support multi-vendor environments, but AI-assisted control loops face challenges in safety, verifiability, and auditability. Existing approaches address agentic coordination, formal intent constraints, zero-trust verification, and cryptographic accountability in isolation, leaving gaps in pre-realization safety and continuous semantic verification. Z^2-ACT integrates these primitives across non-real-time and near-real-time RICs to provide a comprehensive solution.

## [CoAnchor: Anchor-Centric Alignment for Robust Collaborative Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21055) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoAnchor proposes an anchor-centric spatio-temporal alignment framework for asynchronous collaborative perception, using object-level anchors to jointly correct pose noise and communication delays. It integrates spatial refinement, temporal propagation, and current-time verification in a unified loop, achieving robustness under joint perturbations. This addresses a critical practical problem in collaborative perception for autonomous driving: spatio-temporal misalignment caused by communication delays and pose noise. By jointly handling spatial and temporal issues, CoAnchor improves robustness and efficiency, potentially advancing real-world multi-agent perception systems. CoAnchor builds sparse object-level spatio-temporal anchors as a shared interface for pose correction, instead of directly reasoning on dense BEV features. Experiments on simulated and real-world datasets show it remains competitive under clean settings and improves robustness under joint delay and pose perturbations with a favorable accuracy-efficiency trade-off.

**Background**: Collaborative perception in autonomous driving extends a vehicle's sensing range by fusing observations from nearby agents. However, real-world communication delays and relative-pose noise cause stale observations and spatial misalignment, which existing methods typically address from either the spatial or temporal side. CoAnchor introduces an anchor-centric approach to handle both jointly, leveraging object-level anchors as a lightweight interface for alignment.
