AT-ViT, a dual-branch Vision Transformer, introduces a multi-scale, multi-view cross-attention fusion scheme and a mask-guided patch weighting mechanism to improve plant trait recognition from herbarium images. It achieves consistent accuracy gains and significantly better attention localization on plant regions compared to existing models. This work addresses the shortcut learning problem in plant trait recognition, where models rely on background cues instead of plant morphology. By improving robustness and interpretability, AT-ViT could enhance automated plant science research and enable more reliable trait extraction from large herbarium collections. AT-ViT improves plant-region alignment (Avg IoU_p: +15.66 to +18.03 pp) and reduces background overlap (Avg IoU_b: -27.92 to -31.02 pp) relative to CrossViT. It also outperforms ResNet101 by up to +32.32 accuracy points and CrossViT by up to +5.07 points under background-noise conditions.

AT-ViT combines these ideas for plant trait recognition from herbarium scans.

## [PromptResponse: JSON Formatting Boosts LLM Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21074) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper presents a controlled study evaluating how different prompt formats (baseline, JSON, Markdown, YAML, and LLM-tuned) affect GPT-4o's code generation performance, efficiency, and stability across 8,200 executions on five variants of the HumanEval dataset. Results show that consistent formatting, especially JSON, improves generation efficiency and syntactic stability with minor performance gains, while LLM-tuned prompts significantly degrade task performance. This study provides empirical evidence that simple, low-effort prompt reformatting can yield measurable improvements in LLM code generation, offering practical guidance for prompt engineering in software development and research workflows. It also highlights the risks of LLM-based prompt tuning, which can degrade performance if not carefully aligned with the model. The study uses five semantically identical but syntactically distinct variants of the HumanEval dataset: baseline, JSON, Markdown, YAML, and an LLM-tuned version. GPT-4o was used for all 8,200 executions, and the evaluation focused on task performance, generation efficiency, and syntactic stability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: HumanEval is a widely used benchmark for evaluating code generation models, consisting of 164 hand-written programming problems with function signatures, docstrings, and unit tests. Prompt engineering is a critical practice for improving LLM outputs, and prompt tuning refers to optimizing prompts (rather than model weights) to enhance performance. This study contributes to understanding how prompt formatting affects LLM behavior in coding tasks.

## [AID-Guard: Stateful Authorization Protocol for AI Agent Effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21159) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AID-Guard introduces a stateful authorization-to-effect closure protocol that ensures one reservation yields at most one effect across retries and recovery for delegated AI agent actions. The paper presents a Python/SQLite prototype and evaluates it across multiple provider contracts and failure schedules. This protocol addresses a critical gap in AI agent security by binding authorization to actual effects, preventing duplicate or unauthorized actions during retries and recovery. It could enhance the safety and reliability of AI agents in production, particularly in financial and communication services. The prototype blocked 44/44 attacks and admitted 44/44 legitimate proposals under complete proposer compromise. It reduced benign utility by 35.4 to 43.8 percentage points in its strict profile, but a typed frontier recovered 9-10 completions without observed unsafe effects.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents often use tools that cause effects on external systems, but authorization typically only occurs at admission, not at the point of effect. This can lead to issues like duplicate effects from retries or unauthorized changes if the request is modified before commit. AID-Guard aims to close this gap by revalidating at commit and managing reservations.

## [CAIR: Curriculum-Aware Imputation for Physiological Time Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21207) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CAIR, a two-stage imputation framework combining a bidirectional-GRU interpolator with a Transformer refiner that corrects its estimate over three passes, trained under a random-gap curriculum. It outperforms existing methods on continuous glucose monitoring (AI-READI) and arterial pressure (MIMIC-III) under all missingness mechanisms, with margins from 9% to 19% over the strongest baseline. This work addresses a critical gap in clinical data imputation, where generic learned imputers often underperform simple linear interpolation on realistic missingness. By explicitly modeling extreme-value gaps and varying gap lengths, CAIR improves accuracy and preserves clinical burden metrics, potentially enhancing downstream clinical decision-making and research. CAIR couples a bidirectional-GRU interpolator with a Transformer refiner that performs three successive correction passes, trained jointly under a signal-agnostic random-gap curriculum. The evaluation is stratified by gap length and missingness mechanism (MCAR, MAR, NMAR), showing CAIR's largest gains under value-dependent dropout (NMAR), where generic imputers are weakest.

The paper introduces AdaptedKG, a method that derives calibrated knowledge graph (KG) evidence for each training example to denoise user interactions in sequential recommendation, without adding graph representations to the recommendation model. It uses structurally matched alternatives and reference items to compute retention coefficients that gate historical representations and reweight target losses. This work addresses a key challenge in sequential recommendation: distinguishing informative interactions from noisy ones. By leveraging KG evidence without altering the backbone model, it offers a practical and effective denoising approach that can improve recommendation accuracy and robustness, potentially benefiting both researchers and practitioners in recommender systems. AdaptedKG computes all sample-specific scores offline using training interactions and a fixed KG, so the backbone remains unchanged and no KG access is required at inference. Experiments show gains with a standard sequential recommender and multiple behavior-denoising sequential recommenders, indicating its versatility and effectiveness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Sequential recommendation predicts the next item a user will interact with based on their past interactions. However, real user logs contain noisy interactions such as accidental clicks or temporary interests, which can distort the model's understanding. Knowledge graphs (KGs) provide structured relationships between items, offering additional evidence to identify relevant interactions, but raw KG connectivity can be biased by popularity and coverage issues. AdaptedKG addresses this by calibrating KG evidence to better reflect true user preferences.

This enables streaming inference and personalized context lists for multiple users simultaneously. This work addresses a critical production need for ASR systems: accurate recognition of user-specific phrases under strict latency constraints. By supporting streaming and batched decoding with per-stream context biasing, it enables personalized ASR at scale, which is highly relevant for real-world applications like voice assistants and dictation. TurboBias 2.0 supports both offline and streaming inference, and works with greedy and beam-search decoding. The framework allows each utterance in a batch to use an independent context-biasing configuration, preventing context list mixing between users.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Context-biasing is a technique used in ASR to improve recognition of rare, personalized, or domain-specific terms by altering model predictions using external cues. Transducer-based ASR models, such as RNN-T, are widely used in production due to their streaming capabilities. TurboBias is a previous GPU-accelerated context-biasing method that uses a phrase-boosting tree, and TurboBias 2.0 extends it for streaming and batched scenarios.

## [Online Design of Dynamic Networks via Monte Carlo Tree Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08875) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel method for the online design of dynamic networks, using rolling horizon optimization combined with Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS). The approach is demonstrated on a futuristic public transport network where bus lines are constructed on the fly to adapt to stochastic user demand. This work addresses a gap in network design research, which has traditionally been done offline, by enabling networks to adapt in real-time to dynamic and stochastic environments. The application to public transport could lead to more efficient and responsive transit systems, potentially improving performance over traditional vehicle routing methods. The method is compared against state-of-the-art dynamic vehicle routing problem (VRP) resolution methods using a New York City taxi dataset. Unlike classic VRP methods that extend vehicle trajectories in isolation, the proposed method builds a structured network of line buses, enabling complex user journeys and increasing system performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Network design typically occurs offline during a planning phase, but dynamic networks that evolve over time require adaptive strategies. Rolling horizon optimization (RHO) is a sequential decision framework that solves finite-horizon problems iteratively as new information arrives. Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm that combines tree search with random sampling, often used in decision-making and game playing. This paper integrates these techniques to address online network design.

## [ACQ: Two-Stage Framework for Automated Creative Quota Allocation in Online Advertising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06167) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces ACQ, a deployed two-stage framework for automatically allocating creative quotas in large-scale online advertising. It uses a multi-task model based on an unbalanced binary tree to predict quota-conditioned revenue and solves the allocation as a multiple-choice knapsack problem, achieving a 6.20% increase in platform advertising revenue on Kuaishou. This work addresses a practical and under-explored problem in online advertising: determining the optimal number of creatives per photo to maximize revenue under diminishing returns. The deployed system demonstrates significant revenue gains, making it highly relevant to applied ML and systems research in the ad tech industry. The framework consists of two stages: a prediction module (UBTM) that uses an unbalanced binary tree to handle skewed revenue distribution across quota levels, and an allocation module (DBSSolver) that solves the multiple-choice knapsack problem with a dual-based algorithm. Extensive offline and online experiments on Kuaishou's platform validate its effectiveness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: In digital advertising, demand-side platforms (DSPs) allow advertisers to create multiple ad creatives from a single photo for real-time bidding. While more creatives can improve bidding opportunities, the incremental revenue diminishes as the number increases, so DSPs need to automatically determine an appropriate creative quota for each photo. The ACQ framework tackles this by predicting quota-conditioned revenue and optimizing allocation under global capacity constraints.

<a id="item-226"></a>
## [Philosophical Defense of LLM Cognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13988) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper 'Going Whole Hog: A Philosophical Defense of AI Cognition' defends the 'Whole Hog Thesis', arguing that sophisticated LLMs like ChatGPT are full-blown linguistic and cognitive agents with understanding, beliefs, desires, knowledge, and intentions. It proposes a novel methodology based on high-level behavioral observations and 'Holistic Network Assumptions' rather than low-level computational details. This work challenges prevailing methodologies in AI philosophy and could influence debates on AI agency, ethics, and legal responsibility. It provides a systematic rebuttal of common objections, potentially shifting how researchers and policymakers view LLM capabilities. The paper explicitly excludes consciousness from its scope, focusing on cognitive states like beliefs and intentions. It addresses 'Games of Lacks' by arguing that purported necessary conditions for cognition (e.g., semantic grounding, embodiment) are either not truly necessary or are met by LLMs, often using anti-discriminatory comparisons to human capacities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: The 'Whole Hog Thesis' is a philosophical position asserting that LLMs possess full cognitive agency. The paper argues against two common starting points: the 'Just an X' fallacy (dismissing LLMs based on low-level mechanics) and pre-existing theories of mind. Instead, it advocates starting from simple observations of LLM behavior and using 'Holistic Network Assumptions' to infer mental states.

## [Specialized LLM Judges Underperform Unified Training; Deferral Cascades Offer Efficient Alternative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27984) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper empirically compares specialized judge weights (via LoRA experts) and rule-based deferral policies for LLM evaluation on 99,952 rubric-conditioned samples, finding that scratch specialization yields no gains and underperforms unified training by 10.05 accuracy points. In contrast, a lightweight deferral cascade on RewardBench 2 achieves 89.40% accuracy versus 84.75% for a standalone 8B judge at 41.5% compute. These findings challenge the assumption that specialization always improves LLM evaluation, providing practical design rules for building reliable and cost-effective evaluators. This is significant for the growing field of agentic systems and LLM-as-a-judge, where efficient and accurate evaluation is critical. The performance loss from scratch specialization is independent of model size and training settings, but most can be recovered by warm-starting experts from a unified judge. The deferral cascade uses lightweight heads (0.6B-4B-8B) with no core scoring modification, and margin-based deferral matches accuracy at much higher compute cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM evaluators (judges) are used to assess outputs of agentic systems, but they are often slow and expensive. Specialization via LoRA experts aims to improve accuracy by training separate experts for different criteria, while deferral policies allow a small model to decide when to defer to a larger one, forming a cascade. This paper explores when each strategy is beneficial.

## [MemWM: Memory-Augmented World Model Boosts Text-Based Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07107) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MemWM introduces a memory-augmented text-based world model that uses a curated memory bank of transition rules, state caches, and hard-to-predict facts to condition next-state imagination. It also proposes a new evaluation metric, Structured State Fidelity (SSF), and demonstrates significant improvements in factual state preservation and planning performance across ALFWorld, WebShop, and ScienceWorld. This work addresses a known limitation of world models—systematic prediction errors that omit task-critical facts or apply incorrect transition rules—which can hinder planning in text-based environments. By improving factual state fidelity and downstream success rates, MemWM could enhance the reliability of AI agents in interactive tasks, benefiting applications in robotics, game AI, and autonomous decision-making. Compared with supervised fine-tuning (SFT), memory-augmented training improves SSF by up to 206.3%. In the full planning setting, the policy model is kept frozen, and memory-augmented agents achieve up to a 65.4% relative gain in downstream success over an SFT-trained world-model agent. Sensitivity analyses show that retrieved memory improves task success and efficiency under different memory and action-budget settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: World models are used in AI agents to predict how environment states evolve in response to actions, enabling planning. However, they often produce fluent but factually incorrect predictions, especially in text-based environments where task-critical details matter. MemWM augments the world model with a memory bank derived from experience, providing grounding for imagination. The paper also introduces Structured State Fidelity (SSF) to evaluate factual state preservation, addressing the lack of such metrics.

## [TRACE-Memory: Selective Personalization via Utility-Aware Evidence Admission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08446) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRACE-Memory introduces a two-stage framework for selective personalization in generation systems, where Stage 1 retrieves user-specific evidence missing from public context and Stage 2 admits only evidence with incremental utility, potentially returning an empty set. The framework is trained with progressive methods including structured SFT initialization, reduced-space stage-wise GRPO warm-up, and nested multi-sample Joint GRPO. This work addresses a critical gap in retrieval-augmented generation by moving beyond relevance-based retrieval to utility-based evidence admission, enabling more efficient and personalized responses. It demonstrates consistent improvements over random, lexical, and semantic retrieval baselines across diverse tasks, and remains competitive with frontier-LLM pipelines, suggesting potential for more selective and context-aware personalization in production systems. The framework was evaluated on 4,500 Controlled and Natural tasks from Goodreads, Amazon Reviews, and Reddit, consistently outperforming random and lexical memory use and improving over semantic retrieval. The training pipeline includes structured SFT initialization, reduced-space stage-wise GRPO warm-up, and nested multi-sample Joint GRPO, which progressively refine the query-generation and evidence-admission policies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique that enables large language models to retrieve and incorporate external information into their responses. Traditional RAG systems retrieve based on relevance to the query, but TRACE-Memory argues that relevant history may not always add utility, especially if it duplicates public information or concerns a different preference aspect. The framework conditions evidence admission on public-context sufficiency, supporting selective rather than default personalization.

## [Inverse Theory of Mind Pipeline for Recommender Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11354) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces an Inverse Theory of Mind (IToM) pipeline that infers user beliefs and preferences from observed interactions to improve content recommendation in dynamic interfaces. It evaluates the approach on the OPeRA dataset, showing that inferred personas match or exceed ground-truth personas and that multi-hypothesis reasoning is essential for accurate personality prediction. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of current recommender systems, which treat observed actions as reliable preference proxies, by enabling deeper, modality-agnostic user understanding. It has potential impact on future adaptive interfaces, including generative UIs and extended reality (XR), where systems must decide not only what to present but also where, when, and why. The pipeline reconstructs each user's decision context, applies LLM-driven counterfactual reasoning to produce evidence-grounded natural-language belief statements, and synthesizes these beliefs through multi-hypothesis abductive inference into a structured user persona. It is evaluated on four tasks: next action prediction, shopping attitude alignment, Big Five personality inference, and held-out category prediction, and demonstrates cross-modal transferability with a persona-driven spatial banking application on VisionOS.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Recommender systems traditionally rely on observed user actions as proxies for preferences, but these actions often reflect exploration or comparison rather than stable preferences. As interfaces evolve toward generative UIs and XR, there is a growing need for deeper user understanding. Inverse Theory of Mind (IToM) is a novel approach that reasons backward from actions to infer underlying beliefs and preferences, leveraging LLMs for counterfactual reasoning and abductive inference to build user personas.

## [Preprocessing Invariance in Spectral Models Often Just Normalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14227) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper revisits how preprocessing invariance is measured in spectral foundation models, using a Raman foundation model as a case study. It shows that invariance can be trivially achieved by input normalization alone, and should be attributed to normalization rather than learned parameters. This finding challenges the evaluation methodology in spectral foundation models, potentially affecting how future models are assessed and compared. It clarifies that some claimed learned invariances are actually artifacts of normalization, which could lead to more accurate reporting of model capabilities. The paper demonstrates that for a normalization using each spectrum's own statistics, invariance occurs exactly when one spectrum is a positive multiple of the other plus a constant. On six Raman evaluation datasets, the model does not measurably outperform its own normalization, and a controlled experiment shows the encoder learns to ignore a transformation only when it reaches the encoder.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Spectral foundation models are large pre-trained models designed to process spectral data, such as Raman or infrared spectra, for tasks like classification. Preprocessing invariance refers to a model's ability to maintain performance when input preprocessing pipelines differ, which is often desired for robustness across laboratories. Normalization is a common preprocessing step that scales or shifts data, and this paper shows that some normalizations can trivially remove certain transformations, making invariance appear learned when it is not.

## [DECOWAM: Decoupled Whole-Body World-Action Model for Legged Mobile Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DECOWAM introduces a decoupled whole-body world-action model that separates camera ego-motion from base and arm actions, improving action prediction by 21.7% over FastWAM. It also introduces ARMDOG, a real-robot dataset for legged mobile manipulation. This work addresses a critical gap in world-action models, which were primarily designed for fixed-base platforms and fail to account for moving viewpoints in mobile manipulation. By improving action prediction and whole-body coordination, DECOWAM could advance embodied AI and robotics applications involving legged robots. DECOWAM freezes an adapted FastWAM backbone and trains residual adapters, an action-equivalent future bottleneck distilled from privileged observations, adversarially separated base and arm latents, and base-velocity conditioning for video prediction. It uses 25.95M trainable adaptation parameters and achieves a 21.7% reduction in action MSE, with task completion comparable to the strongest baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: World-action models (WAMs) predict future observations and actions jointly, but existing models are mostly designed for fixed-base robots and do not distinguish camera ego-motion from base and arm actions. Legged mobile manipulation requires predicting how locomotion and arm motion jointly alter future observations, which is challenging due to moving viewpoints. DECOWAM builds on FastWAM, a WAM that uses video world-modeling during training but predicts actions directly at inference time.

## [Graphon Particle Systems: Distributed Stochastic Optimization with Convergence Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02765) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes stochastic gradient descent and gradient tracking algorithms for distributed optimization over graphons, a continuum limit of large-scale networked systems. It proves uniform boundedness of second moments and convergence to consensus and optimality under strong convexity. This work provides rigorous theoretical foundations for distributed optimization in the continuum limit, which is crucial for understanding and designing scalable algorithms for massive networks. It extends classical algorithms to graphon settings, potentially impacting areas like multi-agent systems, sensor networks, and machine learning over large graphs. The paper establishes a general lemma for upper bounding a class of time-varying differential inequalities with negative linear terms. For the stochastic gradient tracking algorithm, it develops a decoupling method to analyze coupled nonlinear differential inequalities with time-varying coefficients, showing L^∞-consensus and convergence to the global minimizer in mean square.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Graphons are symmetric measurable functions that represent limits of dense graphs, often used to model large-scale networks. Distributed optimization over graphons generalizes the finite-node problem to a continuum, where each node has a private cost function and the goal is to minimize the integral of these functions. This paper extends stochastic gradient descent and gradient tracking, common algorithms in distributed optimization, to this continuum setting.

## [New Methods Tackle Within-Class Variation in Alzheimer's Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16322) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces two methods, Soft Target Distillation (SoTD) and Instance-level Re-balancing (InRe), to model within-class variation in Alzheimer's disease (AD) detection by estimating sample-specific AD class probabilities. Experiments on ADReSS and CU-MARVEL corpora show improved AD detection performance and alignment with independent cognitive assessments. This work addresses a critical yet underexplored issue in medical machine learning: within-class variation, where patients with the same diagnosis exhibit different severity levels. By improving classification performance and providing insights into modeling such variation, it could lead to more accurate and personalized AD detection tools, benefiting both clinicians and researchers. The proposed methods estimate sample-specific AD class probabilities as sample scores, addressing within-class heterogeneity and instance-level imbalance. The estimated scores align with independent cognitive assessments, and the methods improve AD detection performance on standard corpora, suggesting their potential for broader medical ML applications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Alzheimer's disease (AD) detection typically uses machine learning classification to distinguish between individuals with and without AD. Unlike conventional classification tasks, AD detection involves substantial within-class variation, as individuals with the same diagnosis may have different degrees of cognitive impairment. Soft target distillation (SoTD) uses a KL divergence loss between teacher and student log probabilities, while instance-level re-balancing (InRe) dynamically adjusts sampling probabilities based on instance difficulty. These techniques help model the variation under binary supervision.

This work demonstrates a practical application of multi-agent LLM systems to AI ethics, a timely and critical area as AI systems increasingly impact society. This paper introduces a largely automated one-shot bird call classification pipeline that uses embedding spaces and cosine similarity to detect rare species with minimal training data. The approach was validated on the critically endangered tooth-billed pigeon, achieving 1.0 recall and 0.95 accuracy. This work addresses a critical gap in bioacoustics and conservation technology by enabling classification of rare species with only 1-3 known recordings, which is essential for monitoring the last remaining individuals of endangered birds. It provides a practical, open-source tool for conservationists, potentially improving conservation efforts for species on the brink of extinction. The pipeline leverages the embedding space of large bird classification networks like BirdNET and Perch, combined with filtering and denoising preprocessing, to optimize detection with minimal data. The final model was tested on the tooth-billed pigeon, which has no existing classifiers and only three confirmed recordings, achieving 1.0 recall and 0.95 accuracy.

## [WeedNet: Foundation Model-Based Real-Time Weed Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.18930) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WeedNet, a new AI pipeline, uses self-supervised learning and fine-tuning to identify 1,593 weed species with 91.02% accuracy, and a local Iowa model achieves 97.38% accuracy for 84 regional weeds. This approach addresses the lack of expert-verified data and variable weed morphology, enabling scalable, accurate weed identification for agriculture. The global-to-local fine-tuning strategy could improve robotic weed control and intelligent agricultural consulting. The global model achieves 41% of species with 100% accuracy, and testing shows that image diversity across growth stages is crucial. Validation on drone and ground-rover images suggests potential integration into robotic platforms, and the model can be combined with conversational AI for consulting tools.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Self-supervised learning (SSL) trains models using data itself to generate supervisory signals, reducing the need for labeled data. Fine-tuning adapts a pre-trained model to a specific task with less data, which is a form of transfer learning. Foundation models are large pre-trained models that can be adapted to various downstream tasks.

## [How Humans Perceive RL Agents' Learning Processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13583) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel observation-based paradigm to directly assess human inferences about reinforcement learning agents' learning processes, identifying four core themes through two studies (N=9 and N=34). This work addresses a critical gap in human-AI interaction by providing a human-centered understanding of how people interpret RL agents' learning, which can inform the design of more interpretable and transparent RL systems, especially for collaborative teaching and human-robot interaction. The exploratory interview study (N=9) identified four themes: Agent Goals, Knowledge, Decision Making, and Learning Mechanisms. The confirmatory study (N=34) used an expanded paradigm across two tasks (navigation/manipulation) and two RL algorithms (tabular/function approximation), analyzing 816 responses to validate reliability and refine the thematic framework.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) agents learn through trial and error, but their learning behaviors can be opaque to human observers. Tabular RL methods handle small state-action spaces using tables, while function approximation methods, such as neural networks, scale to larger problems. Understanding how humans perceive these learning processes is essential for effective human-AI collaboration.

## [GeoExplain: New Dataset for Explainable Geo-Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.16633) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GeoExplain is a new dataset and task introduced for explainable geo-localization, comprising 40,350 street-view panorama-location-explanation tuples. The paper also presents SightSense, a multimodal and multilevel reasoning method that predicts location and generates comprehensive explanations. This work addresses a gap in multimodal reasoning evaluation by focusing on hierarchical visual clues at different granularities, which is common in human reasoning. It provides a challenging benchmark that could drive progress in explainable AI and geo-localization, benefiting applications like autonomous navigation and augmented reality. The dataset is collected from the geo-localization game GeoGuessr, and each sample includes panoramic images, geo-coordinates, and human-expert explanations. SightSense demonstrates outstanding performance on GeoExplain, according to the paper's analysis and experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal reasoning involves understanding and integrating information across different data modalities, such as vision and language. Existing tasks for evaluating multimodal reasoning often overlook the hierarchical nature of visual information, where local details and global context both play crucial roles. GeoExplain aims to bridge this gap by requiring models to reason over both levels of granularity in street view images. This is particularly relevant for geo-localization, where identifying a location often relies on combining fine-grained cues like signs with broader context like landscape.

## [SCOPE: Training-Free Generative Prompt Compression via Chunk Rewriting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15813) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SCOPE is a training-free generative prompt compression framework that splits prompts into semantically coherent chunks and rewrites them to be more concise, then reconstructs a meaningful prompt. It outperforms selective compression baselines across most settings, especially at high compression ratios. This addresses the high inference cost and latency of feeding long contexts to LLMs, a practical bottleneck for real-world applications. By being training-free, it offers a lightweight solution that can be easily adopted without additional model training, potentially reducing operational costs and enabling longer context handling. SCOPE uses a chunking-and-summarization mechanism with optimization techniques to preserve critical information and text coherence, and provides finer-grained control of the compression ratio. It was evaluated on question-answering and summarization tasks, showing consistent gains over selective compression baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt compression reduces the length of LLM input to lower computational cost and latency while maintaining generation quality. Traditional methods often remove tokens selectively, which can lose important information; generative approaches like SCOPE rewrite content to be more concise, potentially preserving semantics better. Training-free methods avoid the need for fine-tuning, making them more accessible for practical deployment.

## [SKILL-RAG: Using Self-Knowledge to Filter Retrievals in RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20377) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SKILL-RAG introduces a reinforcement learning-based framework that leverages a model's self-knowledge to filter out irrelevant retrieved documents at the sentence level, improving RAG performance. It was evaluated on Llama2-7B and Qwen3-8B across several question answering benchmarks. This work addresses a key challenge in RAG—hallucinations caused by irrelevant retrieved content—by explicitly using the model's internal knowledge to guide filtering. It offers a novel way to integrate internal and external knowledge, potentially improving the reliability of RAG systems across various applications. The method employs sentence-level granularity to filter out irrelevant content while preserving useful knowledge, and uses a reinforcement learning-based training framework to elicit self-knowledge. Experimental results show that SKILL-RAG not only improves generation quality but also significantly reduces the number of input documents.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) enhances large language models (LLMs) by incorporating external knowledge from retrieved documents, but irrelevant content can lead to hallucinations. Self-knowledge refers to what a model 'knows' and 'does not know,' which can be used to judge the usefulness of retrieved information. SKILL-RAG builds on this idea by using reinforcement learning to train the model to filter based on its self-knowledge.

## [Perseus: Interactive Time Series Segmentation with Sparse Supervision via Stateful Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09930) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Perseus is a new framework for interactive time series segmentation that uses a stateful memory bank to retain sparse user corrections across unprompted gaps, improving segmentation accuracy without retraining. It transitions from synchronous processing to asynchronous state management with a Write-Read architecture, achieving up to 85% accuracy improvement in multi-granularity settings. This work addresses a practical limitation of stateless baselines in interactive segmentation, where user feedback is often grouped around specific events, leaving gaps. By enabling persistent memory of interactions, Perseus can significantly improve performance in real-world applications like industrial monitoring and healthcare, where expert corrections are sparse but crucial. Perseus decouples supervision from inference via a Write-Read architecture: grouped user cues are asynchronously encoded into a persistent memory bank (Write), which is then actively queried by the inference engine (Read) to service unprompted windows. Extensive experiments on six datasets show that stateless prompting baselines degrade significantly under grouped supervision, while Perseus maintains robustness. Code and preprocessing instructions are available at https://github.com/blacksnail789521/Perseus.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Time series segmentation involves dividing a time series into meaningful segments or states, which is crucial in fields like industrial monitoring and healthcare. Traditional approaches often require dense labels for training, but interactive segmentation allows sparse expert prompts to correct predictions at inference time. However, many existing methods are stateless, meaning they only use guidance within the current window and cannot retain information across gaps in user feedback. Perseus introduces stateful memory to overcome this limitation, enabling more effective use of sparse supervision.

## [GroupSegment-SHAP: Shapley Explanations for Multivariate Time Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces GroupSegment-SHAP (GS-SHAP), a novel Shapley-based explanation method for multivariate time series that constructs explanatory units as group-segment players based on cross-variable dependence and temporal distribution shifts. It is evaluated across four real-world domains and shows improved faithfulness and reduced runtime compared to existing time-series SHAP variants. GS-SHAP addresses a critical gap in time-series interpretability by jointly modeling cross-variable interactions and temporal dynamics, which existing SHAP variants often treat independently. This could significantly improve trust and usability of complex time-series models in high-stakes domains like healthcare, finance, and energy. GS-SHAP improves deletion-based faithfulness (DeltaAUC) by about 1.7x on average over time-series SHAP baselines, while reducing wall-clock runtime by about 40% under matched perturbation budgets. The method is compared against KernelSHAP, TimeSHAP, SequenceSHAP, WindowSHAP, and TSHAP across human activity recognition, power-system forecasting, medical signal analysis, and financial time series.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) is a widely used game-theoretic approach for interpreting machine learning models by assigning each feature an importance value. For multivariate time series, existing SHAP variants often treat feature and time axes independently, which can fragment structural signals formed jointly by multiple variables over specific intervals. GS-SHAP constructs explanatory units as group-segment players, combining cross-variable dependence and temporal distribution shifts, to better capture these joint interactions.

## [PhotoBench: New Benchmark for Personalized Intent-Driven Photo Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01493) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PhotoBench introduces the first benchmark for personalized photo retrieval built from authentic personal albums, shifting the focus from visual matching to multi-source intent-driven reasoning. It integrates visual semantics, spatial-temporal metadata, social identity, and temporal events to synthesize complex queries. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in existing retrieval benchmarks, which rely on context-isolated web snapshots and fail to capture the multi-source reasoning needed for authentic user queries. It highlights the limitations of current unified embedding models and points toward the need for robust agentic reasoning systems, impacting the future of personal multimodal retrieval. The benchmark includes three distinct personal albums representing different user archetypes, and its evaluation reveals two critical limitations: the modality gap, where unified embedding models fail on non-visual constraints, and the source fusion paradox, where agentic systems struggle with tool orchestration. The PhotoBench dataset is publicly available.

## [DiSE: Self-Evaluation for Diffusion Language Models via Token Regeneration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02760) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DiSE, a self-evaluation method for diffusion large language models (dLLMs) that quantifies confidence by computing the probability of regenerating tokens in the generated sequence. It also proposes a flexible-length generation framework that adaptively controls sequence length based on the model's self-assessment. This work addresses a key challenge in quality assessment for diffusion-based language models, which are gaining attention for their diversity and parallelism but lack effective self-evaluation methods. DiSE could enable more reliable output quality control and adaptive generation, impacting the growing field of diffusion LLMs. DiSE leverages token regeneration probabilities for likelihood estimation and uncertainty quantification, and is validated through experiments on likelihood evaluation, uncertainty quantification, and flexible-length generation. The method is shown to be positively correlated with semantic coherence and answer accuracy, and its feasibility is analyzed from the perspective of dLLM generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) generate text through a non-sequential, bidirectionally masked process, which makes quality assessment difficult compared to autoregressive models. Self-evaluation methods typically rely on likelihood or uncertainty estimates, but these are not straightforward for dLLMs. DiSE addresses this by using the probability of regenerating tokens given the full context, providing a practical confidence measure.

## [InverFill: One-Step Inversion Boosts Few-Step Diffusion Inpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23463) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InverFill introduces a one-step inversion method that injects semantic information from the masked input into the initial noise, enabling high-fidelity few-step diffusion inpainting without training. It significantly improves vanilla blended sampling and matches specialized inpainting models at low NFE. This addresses the practical limitation of slow sampling in diffusion inpainting, making it more applicable to real-world use. It offers a training-free solution that boosts existing few-step models, potentially accelerating adoption in image editing and content creation. InverFill uses a blended sampling pipeline with semantically aligned noise, avoiding real-image supervision and adding minimal inference overhead. Experiments show consistent improvements in image quality and text coherence across baseline few-step models.

**Background**: Diffusion models achieve photorealistic inpainting but require many sampling steps, limiting practical use. Few-step text-to-image models offer faster generation, but naive application to inpainting causes artifacts due to random Gaussian noise initialization. InverFill addresses this by injecting semantic information into the initial noise, improving harmonization and fidelity.

## [ChemGraph-XANES: LLM Agentic Framework Automates XANES Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16205) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ChemGraph-XANES, an LLM-based agentic framework, automates XANES simulation workflows by integrating RAG, schema-constrained tool execution, deterministic FDMNES input generation, Parsl-backed execution, and provenance-aware data curation. It achieved end-to-end completion in 10/10 composition-based runs, 10/10 structure-file-based runs, and 9/10 documentation-grounded RAG runs. This framework significantly reduces the complexity of XANES simulations, enabling researchers to perform large-scale computational spectroscopy campaigns with minimal manual intervention. It demonstrates the potential of LLM agents in scientific computing, paving the way for more accessible and reproducible materials science workflows. The framework supports three workflow modes: documentation-grounded parameter propagation, structure-file-based execution, and composition-based execution. In a task-parallel demonstration, it retrieved 21 TiO2 structures from the Materials Project and successfully submitted one FDMNES calculation per structure, with Parsl distributing tasks across the worker pool.

## [Cyclic Subtask Graphs for LLM Agents: Flexibility vs. Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22820) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces complete cyclic subtask graphs for tool-using LLM agents, where a unified state-analysis-and-routing agent selects transitions based on natural-language criteria. It evaluates task-specific (Spec-Cyc) and benchmark-generic (Gen-Cyc) cyclic graphs against ReAct and dependency-directed workflows (DepDAG) on TextCraft, ALFWorld, and Finance-Agent. The findings challenge the assumption that more flexible workflows are always better, showing that cyclic routing can add overhead in some tasks while improving success in others. This provides a diagnostic framework for practitioners to decide when to use cyclic graphs versus simpler controllers, impacting the design of long-horizon LLM agent systems. The paper identifies three workflow signatures: prerequisite-chain (TextCraft), partially observable recovery (ALFWorld), and open-ended evidence-synthesis (Finance-Agent). It also includes fault-injection robustness analysis, token-cost accounting, graph-stability reporting, transition-audit checks for DepDAG, and failure-mode structure analysis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often use workflows to handle long-horizon tasks, where subtasks may need to be revisited. Traditional approaches like ReAct use a simple loop, while dependency-directed workflows (e.g., DAGs) enforce forward constraints. Cyclic graphs allow revisiting any subtask, but add routing and coordination costs. This paper explores when such flexibility is beneficial.

## [PBSD: Reward-Regularized Self-Distillation Beyond KL Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05040) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Preference-Based Self-Distillation (PBSD), a novel reward-regularized approach that replaces fixed-teacher KL matching with a preference-based objective, achieving stronger average performance and improved training stability on mathematical reasoning and tool-use benchmarks. PBSD addresses key limitations of existing self-distillation methods, such as training instability and performance degradation, offering a more robust alternative for on-policy LLM training. This could lead to more efficient and stable self-improvement of language models without relying on external teachers. PBSD derives a reward-regularized objective whose analytic optimum is a reward-reweighted teacher distribution, and optimizes preference gaps between teacher and student samples while maintaining on-policy student sampling. The paper also provides a statistical analysis establishing when on-policy self-distillation is preferable to learning from an external teacher.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation is an efficient alternative to reinforcement learning for training LLMs, providing dense token-level signals. However, traditional methods rely on a stronger external teacher, and recent self-distillation approaches use the same model as both teacher and student under different prompt contexts, but they often reduce to KL matching, which can be unstable and degrade reasoning performance.

## [S-AI-Recursive: Bio-Inspired Sparse AI with Convergent Recursive Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13872) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces S-AI-Recursive, a sparse AI architecture that implements reasoning as a hormonally regulated closed-loop iteration rather than a single feed-forward pass. It provides formal convergence guarantees using Lyapunov analysis and entropic contraction, along with experimental results showing significant reductions in reasoning depth. This work offers a novel bio-inspired approach to recursive reasoning with formal guarantees, potentially improving efficiency and robustness in AI systems. It could influence future research on adaptive reasoning and resource allocation in machine learning models. On convergent Maze instances, adaptive stopping reduced mean depth from 20.00 to 11.31 iterations (43.4% reduction). On compatible recurring Sudoku instances, warm-start reduced mean depth from 18.39 to 2.00 cycles, saving 16.39 cycles. Robustness tests showed advantages over residual-only stopping on deceptive plateaus but not under homogeneous Gaussian noise.

**Background**: Recursive reasoning in AI involves iterative refinement of outputs, often used in meta-prompting and tiny networks. Hormonal regulation in biology uses feedback mechanisms to maintain homeostasis, inspiring this architecture's use of two hormones: Clarifine (convergence signal) and Confusionin (residual-uncertainty signal). ## [Component-Aware Style Transfer for Satellite Sim2Real Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19624) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a component-aware, structure-preserving style transfer framework that generates realistic satellite images from synthetic renders by extracting part-wise real-domain style codes and injecting them into corresponding synthetic regions via mask-aligned modulation. Experiments on 5,000 rendered and 100 real images show improved FID (54.32) and KID (0.048), and downstream pose estimation performance (ADD pass rate 0.260, AUC 0.611). This work addresses a critical challenge in satellite visual Sim2Real data construction, where real annotated data is scarce. By enabling component-level appearance transfer, it improves the realism of synthetic data while preserving annotations, which can enhance downstream tasks like pose estimation in space robotics and autonomous navigation. The framework uses ArUco-based camera pose measurement to build weakly paired real-synthetic samples, and combines adversarial training with local contrastive consistency, self-regularization, and edge-preserving constraints. The downstream GDRNet pose estimator is trained only on synthetic or translated synthetic images, and the method outperforms representative image-translation baselines in the calibrated setup.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Sim2Real data construction aims to bridge the gap between synthetic and real imagery for training computer vision models. In satellite visual sensing, real images with accurate pose labels and component masks are hard to obtain, while synthetic rendering provides exact annotations but suffers from an appearance gap. Style transfer techniques can adapt synthetic images to look more realistic, but preserving structural and component-level details is crucial for downstream tasks. ArUco markers are commonly used for camera pose estimation, providing a reliable way to align real and synthetic views.

## [Chatterbox-Flash: Prior-Calibrated Block Diffusion for Streaming Zero-Shot TTS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30748) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Chatterbox-Flash fine-tunes a pretrained autoregressive TTS decoder into a block-diffusion decoder, enabling parallel token generation within blocks while maintaining streaming. It introduces two inference-time techniques—prior-calibrated scoring and an early-decoding schedule—to mitigate quality degradation from long-tail token distributions. This work bridges the gap between autoregressive and non-autoregressive TTS by enabling streaming with parallel generation, achieving quality comparable to strong baselines. It offers a practical path to low-latency, high-fidelity zero-shot TTS, which is crucial for real-time applications like voice assistants and interactive agents. The method requires no architectural modification, only fine-tuning and inference-time adjustments. On standard benchmarks, it achieves time-to-first-packet on par with streaming AR systems and a substantially lower real-time factor, with code and audio samples available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Zero-shot TTS aims to synthesize speech for unseen speakers from a short reference clip. Autoregressive models generate tokens sequentially, offering high quality but high latency, while diffusion models can generate in parallel but often require full-sequence processing. Block diffusion models interpolate between these by denoising blocks of tokens, enabling partial parallelism. However, applying block diffusion to discrete speech tokens can suffer from long-tail distribution bias, which this paper addresses.

## [Know2Guess: Contamination-Aware Benchmark for LLM Knowledge Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26101) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Know2Guess, a contamination-aware multi-zone benchmark with 1,200 items across five domains, designed to evaluate LLMs' ability to abstain from answering when knowledge is absent. It includes explicit abstention expectations, contamination-risk metadata, and dual parsing with strict and normalized robustness parsers. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by separating supported answering from unsupported guessing, while accounting for data contamination and prompt idiosyncrasy. It provides a reproducible protocol for auditing answerability, abstention, refusal, and contamination as distinct dimensions of LLM reliability, which is essential for building trustworthy AI systems. The benchmark evaluates FLAN-T5, Qwen2.5-Instruct, and Llama-3-Instruct models under locked answer-or-abstain prompts, answer-only controls, and prompt-template variants. Results show that FLAN baselines are weak on productive abstention, while Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct achieves the best overall reliability, but answer-expected zones remain difficult and calibration remains poor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often hallucinate when they lack knowledge, and existing benchmarks often conflate knowledge with guessing or contamination. Abstention, the refusal to answer, is a promising approach to mitigate hallucinations, but evaluating it requires separating cases where the model knows the answer from those where it should abstain. This benchmark introduces a multi-zone design to measure the transition from answerable knowledge to abstention-expected unknowns, with contamination-aware metadata to ensure fair evaluation.

## [LLM Persona: Frame-Dependent Geometry vs. Robust Aggregates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02368) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper reveals that LLM persona expression comprises two dissociable components: aggregated Big Five scores that are frame-robust but degrade under randomization (21% drop), and geometric features on SPD manifolds that collapse under frame misalignment (42% drop) but recover to 84% under shared frames, surpassing aggregated features (76%). This dual-nature framework challenges static trait conceptions of LLM personas and necessitates frame-aware evaluation methods, impacting how researchers and practitioners assess and interpret LLM personality and behavior. The study uses IPIP-50 responses from GPT-4o simulating American and Chinese-American personas, constructing within-instance correlation matrices and analyzing them on SPD manifolds under manipulated question orderings. The collapse-recovery pattern indicates that persona geometry is a frame-dependent coordination pattern encoding information invisible to aggregation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM persona evaluation often relies on psychometric questionnaires like IPIP-50, which measure Big Five traits through aggregate scores. However, this approach discards the correlation structure within a single response instance. SPD manifolds provide a mathematical framework for analyzing such geometric structures, which can reveal patterns not captured by simple averages.

## [SMOPD: Multi-Reward RL via Specialize-and-Merge Online Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03092) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SMOPD, a two-stage training method for multi-reward reinforcement learning that first trains reward-specialized teachers with reward-priority configurations and then merges them into a single student policy via online policy distillation. Experiments show SMOPD outperforms GDPO across 1.5B, 3B, and 7B backbones on complementary and conflicting reward settings. This work addresses a critical challenge in multi-reward RL: balancing dense and sparse rewards to ensure all capabilities are effectively learned. By strengthening sparse reward signals without sacrificing dense reward performance, SMOPD could improve the training of complex models like LLMs, where multiple objectives (e.g., helpfulness and harmlessness) must be optimized simultaneously. SMOPD consists of two stages: Stage 1 (Specialize) trains multiple reward-specialized teachers using reward-priority configurations, and Stage 2 (Merge) uses online policy distillation to combine these teachers into a single student policy. The method is validated on two multi-reward settings: complementary rewards (tool-calling accuracy and format) and conflicting rewards (helpful and harmless rewards), showing consistent improvements over GDPO across model sizes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-reward reinforcement learning (RL) involves optimizing a policy against multiple reward signals, which often have different scales and granularities. Direct scalarization, where rewards are summed, can cause one reward to dominate and mask others. GDPO (Group reward-Decoupled Normalization Policy Optimization) addresses this by normalizing each reward dimension separately before aggregation, but it still struggles when rewards have very different granularities, such as a dense reward with fine-grained scores and a sparse binary reward. SMOPD builds on GDPO by using a specialize-and-merge strategy to ensure each reward provides a sufficient optimization signal.

## [ELVAE: Evidential Learning-Based VAE for Uncertainty-Aware Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ELVAE, a variational autoencoder that integrates evidential learning by placing an input-dependent normal-inverse-gamma (NIG) hierarchy at each latent coordinate, enabling separation of epistemic and aleatoric uncertainty. Theoretical analysis shows that the marginalized latent law identifies only three quotient coordinates, and empirical results demonstrate that the trained inverse evidence 1/ν serves as a strong sensitivity-ranking score. This work bridges evidential learning and variational autoencoders, offering a principled way to quantify and separate different types of uncertainty in generative models. It could enhance interpretability and reliability in applications such as anomaly detection, active learning, and decision-making under uncertainty. The NIG hierarchy separates location uncertainty u_epi = β/[ν(α-1)] from conditional variability u_var = β/(α-1), but the marginalized latent law identifies only three quotient coordinates (γ, α, c) with c = β(1+1/ν). Empirical results show that at τ_epi=1, the three-seed mean high/low-u_epi semantic-transition ratios are 1.98 on MNIST and 1.66 on Fashion-MNIST, falling to 1.33 and 1.16 under scale-matched controls.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Variational autoencoders (VAEs) are generative models that learn latent representations by maximizing an evidence lower bound. Uncertainty quantification in deep learning distinguishes aleatoric uncertainty (inherent noise) from epistemic uncertainty (model uncertainty), which is crucial for reliable predictions. Evidential learning uses conjugate priors like the normal-inverse-gamma distribution to model both types of uncertainty in a principled manner.

## [Skill Representation Affects In-Context Retrieval in Multimodal Agent Harness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20389) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents a case study of Tinycloud, a production multimodal video agent harness, examining how its skill representations (tool-skills and workflow-skills) affect in-context selection by the LLM planner. A six-task ablation across three exposure regimes reveals that full autoload selects the gold skill on every task, while partial exposure can cause lexical competition that suppresses correct selection. This finding challenges the assumption that exposing more skills in the system prompt is always beneficial, highlighting a trade-off in agent design. It connects small-N in-context selection to large-scale embedding-based retrieval, offering practical insights for building more reliable LLM-based agents. The harness exposes skills via two surfaces: an inlined-body surface for autoloaded skills and a one-line listing for on-demand skills. The production default misroutes one task because its lexical signal collides with an autoloaded tool-skill, pulling planner attention away from a listed workflow-skill.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness is a deterministic software layer that wraps a model to translate its textual intent into real-world actions. In LLM-based agents, skills are often selected in-context by the planner from representations in the system prompt, which is the small-N counterpart to embedding-based retrieval at scale. This case study uses Tinycloud, a production multimodal video agent harness, to illustrate how skill representation and exposure affect selection.

This paper introduces CMB-0.1, a benchmark for detecting criterion revision in LLM agents, and evaluates it across twelve cross-domain cases and four arms, finding that no model trial satisfies all five required conditions. It also proposes a successor protocol, CMB-0.4, with a trace-anchored design. This work addresses a nuanced issue in LLM agent evaluation—criterion revision—which is crucial for ensuring agents adhere to broader commitments over time. The negative result and the proposed protocol provide a foundation for more rigorous testing, potentially impacting AI safety and reliability in long-horizon tasks. The five non-compensatory conditions are criterion-failure detection, model-emitted proposal, new-episode transfer, intervention sensitivity, and preservation. The evaluation involved 84 deterministic scorer trials and 192 model-case-arm trials, with Qwen2.5-7B showing zero-state reconstruction failures. The proposed CMB-0.4 protocol includes concealed transfer, explicit WRITE/NO-WRITE/ESCALATE actions, and a frozen executable oracle.

<a id="item-267"></a>
## [Critical Review of LLMs in Hadith Computational Science](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20364) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents a critical narrative review of the application of large language models (LLMs) to hadith computational science, identifying both advances and unresolved challenges. It synthesizes expert perspectives on authenticity, authority, and responsible use, and proposes a research agenda for the field. This review addresses a critical gap in the literature by moving beyond benchmark performance to consider the methodological robustness and scholarly utility of LLM-based hadith computation. It highlights the need for evidence infrastructure and expert supervision, which is essential for the responsible integration of AI into Islamic scholarship. The review identifies uneven progress: data resources have expanded, segmentation tasks have matured, and LLM-assisted workflows support corpus-scale enrichment, but constraints remain in narrow corpora, benchmark comparability, synthetic-to-real transfer, narrator identity resolution, preprocessing fragility, reproducibility, and expert-grounded validation. It also notes gaps in non-canonical corpora, commentary literature, cross-source links, and fiqh-facing evidence support.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Hadith are records of the words, actions, and silent approvals of the Prophet Muhammad, transmitted through chains of narrators. Computational hadith studies apply natural language processing and machine learning to analyze these texts, and recent advances in transformer models and LLMs have opened new possibilities. This review critically assesses how these technologies are being used and what methodological challenges remain.

<a id="item-268"></a>
## [DSR: Knowledge-Graph-Gated Defactualization for Fact-Preserving Style Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20393) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Defactualize-Steer-Rehydrate (DSR), a framework that integrates a typed, salience-weighted knowledge graph with activation steering to enable style-controllable generation while preserving factual correctness in agentic LLMs. DSR replaces salient entities with typed placeholders before steering and restores verified values after generation, evaluated on six LLaMA-family models (1B–13B) across 1,200 generations. This work addresses a critical limitation of activation steering—its inability to distinguish facts from style—by introducing explicit knowledge engineering, which is essential for deploying LLMs in fact-sensitive applications like customer support. It demonstrates a fine-tuning-free approach to enhance trustworthiness and controllability, potentially influencing future agentic AI systems. DSR significantly increases verified-entity recovery compared to a steering-only baseline (Cohen's d=0.225, p_Bonf=1.0×10^-4), though absolute recovery rates remain modest. The framework includes a layered regex/NER/lexical-classifier pipeline for entity extraction and a dedicated KG ablation study, with code and cached steering vectors publicly released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Activation steering is a technique for controlling LLM behavior by modifying hidden states during inference, without fine-tuning. However, it lacks an explicit mechanism to separate factual content from stylistic elements, leading to semantic leakage. Knowledge graphs provide structured representations of entities and relationships, which can be used to identify and preserve factual information. DSR combines these ideas to achieve both style control and fact preservation.

## [Raman Spectroscopy and ML Achieve 100% Accuracy in Edible Oil Authentication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20440) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study combines Raman spectroscopy with machine learning techniques (t-SNE, K-means, Decision Trees, NNLS) to authenticate edible oils, achieving 100% classification accuracy for pure oils using only four spectral variables from the original 1866-feature space. This work demonstrates that highly accurate and interpretable oil authentication is possible with minimal spectral data, paving the way for frugal and edge AI applications in portable food-quality monitoring and fraud prevention. The study used five edible oils in pure form and within a fried-potato-chip matrix. For matrix-containing samples, NNLS-based physics-informed AI decomposition improved classification, achieving 86.4% and 85.4% accuracy for paper-subtracted and paper-plus-potato-subtracted datasets, respectively, while reducing important variables to five and four.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Raman spectroscopy is a vibrational spectroscopy technique commonly used for edible oil authentication because it provides a molecular fingerprint without extensive sample preparation. Machine learning methods like t-SNE help visualize high-dimensional spectral data, while decision trees offer interpretable classification rules. Non-negative least squares (NNLS) is a constrained optimization technique that decomposes spectra into non-negative components, useful for separating overlapping signals from different sources.

## [ExploraTwin: Non-Profit Platform for Digital Twin Survey Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20539) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ExploraTwin is introduced as an open-access, non-profit research platform for digital twin survey simulations, supporting two modes: survey mode and panel mode. It also includes CroissantTwin, a standardized data format for adding digital twin samples. This platform lowers the barrier for researchers and practitioners to test and deploy digital twin simulations, potentially accelerating research in AI/ML and systems. Its high fidelity (99.6% valid responses) suggests it could become a standard tool for survey-based digital twin studies. ExploraTwin supports uploading Qualtrics survey files or creating surveys within the platform, and it demonstrated survey mode by replicating 19 experiments from the Twin-2K-500 dataset. The platform achieved 99.6% structural validity across 197,000 answer units on the first run.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Digital twin simulations use virtual representations of real-world entities to run simulations, and in survey research, they involve using AI-generated personas to simulate human responses. The platform aims to standardize and simplify the process, making it accessible to a wider research community.

## [ID-HOINet: Identity-Aware HOI Motion Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20690) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces the Identity-Aware Human-Object Interaction Motion Captioning task, requiring captions to specify subject identity, and proposes ID-HOINet, which learns from multi-view videos and supports single-view identity-aware caption generation. This work addresses a limitation in existing HOI motion captioning methods that use generic subject terms, enabling more precise and personalized descriptions. It could improve applications in video understanding, human-robot interaction, and assistive technologies. ID-HOINet consists of two core components: Multi-View Identity-Motion Learning Module (MVIML) and Two-Stage Caption Rewriting Strategy (TSCR). The method is evaluated on BEHAVE and InterCap datasets, achieving state-of-the-art performance, and code will be released upon acceptance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Human-object interaction (HOI) motion captioning aims to generate natural language descriptions of human actions involving objects. Previous methods typically refer to the subject as 'a person' or 'someone', lacking identity grounding. This paper introduces identity-aware captioning, which requires specifying the subject's identity, and uses multi-view learning to capture both identity and motion features.

## [Lightweight LLMs Evaluated on 5G Fault Analysis with LLM-as-Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21021) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper evaluates three lightweight LLMs (Claude-Haiku-4.5, GPT-5.4-Mini, Gemini-3.1-Flash-Lite) on free-text 5G domain knowledge and fault analysis tasks, using a multi-judge LLM-as-Judge framework to score open-ended responses. All models achieved at least 90% accuracy on fault diagnosis, but zero-shot recall of 3GPP and O-RAN specifications remained below 60%. This work addresses the need for scalable evaluation of LLMs in telecom fault analysis, moving beyond restrictive multiple-choice benchmarks to free-text generation. The findings highlight the potential of lightweight, edge-deployable models for production telecom deployments, with Gemini-3.1-Flash-Lite offering the best efficiency trade-off. The study uses three benchmarks (TeleQNA, 5G-Faults, TeleInter) and three independent frontier judges to score outputs, with pairwise inter-judge agreement at least 0.90 across all runs. Gemini-3.1-Flash-Lite achieved the lowest inference cost and latency while maintaining competitive accuracy, making it the most suitable for production.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-as-Judge is a framework where large language models are used to evaluate the outputs of other language models, offering a scalable alternative to human evaluation. TeleQnA is a benchmark dataset for assessing LLM knowledge in telecommunications, containing 10,000 questions from standards and research. This paper extends such evaluation to free-text fault analysis, which is more realistic for 5G/6G network diagnostics.

<a id="item-274"></a>
## [ARDLS: Anchored Regularized Direct Least Squares for Unique AHP Priorities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21187) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces the Anchored Regularized Direct Least Squares (ARDLS) optimization model, which integrates established prioritization operators as regularization anchors to guarantee a unique global minimum for priority vectors in the Analytic Hierarchy Process (AHP). This addresses a critical limitation of Direct Least Squares (DLS) in AHP, where multiple solutions lead to unstable rankings. ARDLS provides a mathematically rigorous method to ensure stable and unique priority vectors, potentially improving decision-making reliability across various application domains. ARDLS anchors normalization techniques, the Eigenvector method, Singular Value Decomposition, Cosine Maximization, and the Pseudo-Inverse Gram Matrix (closed-form Weighted Least Squares) within a regularization penalty. Numerical experiments show it reduces root mean square error among established priority operators while guaranteeing strict mathematical uniqueness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: The Analytic Hierarchy Process (AHP) is a decision-making method that uses pairwise reciprocal matrices to derive priority vectors. Direct Least Squares (DLS) is an intuitive approach but suffers from non-convexity, leading to multiple global minima under high inconsistency, such as cyclic contradictions. ARDLS addresses this by incorporating established prioritization operators as anchors to break symmetries and ensure a unique solution.

## [Study: Neural Network Compressibility for Avian Monitoring on MCUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17751) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper investigates how the number of target bird species affects the compressibility of neural networks for deployment on low-power microcontrollers (MCUs). The authors trained and compressed models for various class counts, demonstrating significant compression rates with minimal performance loss. This work enables energy-autonomous avian monitoring by running efficient AI models directly on inexpensive MCUs in the field, reducing costs and computational requirements compared to traditional methods. It addresses the need for scalable wildlife monitoring amid biodiversity loss, potentially impacting conservation efforts and edge AI applications. The study provides benchmarking results across different hardware platforms and evaluates the feasibility of energy-autonomous devices. The paper is a preprint (arXiv:2602.17751v2) with moderate novelty, and no community discussion was provided.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 24, 04:00

**Background**: Passive acoustic monitoring (PAM) records animal sounds over long periods to track species, but traditional analysis requires complex models and substantial computational resources. Microcontrollers (MCUs) are inexpensive, low-power chips that can run machine learning models at the edge, enabling energy-autonomous devices powered by batteries or energy harvesting. Neural network compression techniques, such as pruning and quantization, reduce model size and computational load, making deployment on MCUs feasible.
